In 2019, the NBM paid interest to banks in the
amount of MDL 530.2 million, by 19.8% more than in 2018. The
increase in NBM interest expenses on required reserves was due
to maintaining a high norm of required reserves in MDL during the
year and the increase by 1.0 percentage point of the NBM’s
monetary policy rates in the summer of 2019.
Thus, in the
reporting year the rate of return on required reserves in MDL – the
rate on the overnight deposit facility – was on average 3.9%
compared to 3.5% in 2018. At the same time, during 2019, the
NBM changed the principles of remuneration of required reserves
in foreign currency (USD and EUR), paying banks interest rates of

sector for new demand deposits in foreign currency with interest.
Respectively, in 2019, the NBM remunerated the foreign exchange
required reserves at a lower average annual rate, of 0.12%,
compared to 0.36% in 2018.
Intervention on the domestic foreign exchange market
In 2019, the National Bank of Moldova intervened on the domestic
foreign exchange market both as a buyer and as a seller of foreign
currency to avoid excessive ﬂuctuations in the exchange rate of
the MDL in the context of gaps, both positive and negative between
foreign exchange supply and demand registered on the intrabank
market at different times of the year.

Dynamic of monetary indicators
Throughout 2019, monetary indicators maintained a positive trend.
Thus, the average growth rate of broad money supply recorded
8.2% in annual terms. Other monetary aggregates recorded a
similar behaviour during the reference year.
Money supply
In 2019,
the money supply (M2)21 rose by 8.2%, being 1.9

percentage points below the annual growth rate in 2018 (Chart
3.7). During the ﬁrst half of the year, the evolution of the M2
monetary aggregate was characterized by an upward trend,
peaking in June, after which the growth rate gradually slowed.
Both components of M2 have seen positive developments. Thus,
deposits in national currency and money in circulation increased
by 7.9% and, respectively, by 8.9% compared to 2018.
The deposits in MDL were characterized by a uniform evolution
during the ﬁrst three quarters, the growth rate averaging around
5.7%, with a gradual acceleration in the last quarter. Thus, at the
end of December 2019, the annual growth reached the level of
7.9%. At the same time, it is worth mentioning the year-round
moderation of the increase of deposits in MDL compared to their
evolution in 2018, when the annual increase was 9.8%.
The
evolution of deposits in MDL was largely determined by demand
deposits. At the same time, term deposits generated non-essential
negative contributions throughout the year, except for the last
two months, in which they had signiﬁcant positive contributions.
Throughout the year, the money in circulation had a positive
evolution characterized by an accelerated growth rate in the ﬁrst
half and a more moderate pace in the second half, the growth rate
at the end of the year being 1.7 percentage points below the level
of annual growth in 2018.
At the same time, the money supply (M3)22 recorded an annual
growth of 8.2%, its growth rate having increased by 0.2 percentage
points, compared to 2018 (Chart 3.8).
At the end of 2019, the growth rate of foreign currency deposits
amounted to 8.0%, being determined by the major contribution of
demand deposits. Foreign currency term deposits, recalculated in
MDL, increased by 1.5% per year, whereas sight deposits increased
by 17.6%.
At the end of 2019, the share of domestic currency deposits in
total deposits was similar to that of 2018 and amounted to 60.0%,
and the share of foreign currency deposits – 40.0%.

residents denominated in MDL.

of December 2019, having decreased by 0.5 percentage points
compared to the end of December 2018. Within the structure of
deposits in foreign currency, 56.0% represent term deposits, by
3.6 percentage points below the level recorded in 2018.

rates on the balance of loans and the registration of the liquidity
surplus in the banking system.

recorded 11.5%, higher by 2.8 times than in December 2018 (Chart

At the end of 2019, the balance of loans granted to legal entities,
whose share was 66.4%, recorded a growth rate of 1.7% (compared
to the negative annual rate at the end of 2018). The positive
annual dynamics appeared at the end of February 2019 and was
maintained throughout the reporting year.
Towards the end of 2019, the growth rate of the balance of credits
to individuals amounted to 37.6%.
The dynamics was largely
inﬂuenced by the increase in the balance of loans in MDL, whose
share was about 97.0%, most of which were loans granted for
consumption and real estate, associated with the relatively low
level of interest rates.
In the structure of currencies, the balance of loans in national
currency continued its upward trend started in 2018 throughout
2019, by recording positive double-digit annual rates, due to the
revival of the lending process in MDL and the decrease of the
component rate in currency denominated in US dollars (Chart
3.10). Thus, the annual growth rate of the total balance of loans
granted in the economy in MDL constituted at the end of the
reporting year 19.9%, higher than at the end of 2018 by 3.8 times.
It should be mentioned that, at the end of 2019, the growth rate
of the balance of loans granted in foreign currency recalculated in
MDL was only 0.2%, decreasing by 2.4 percentage points.
Evolution of new granted loans
In 2019, the annual volume of new granted loans recorded an
increase, amounting to MDL 33,833.0 million, having risen by
17.5%, compared to 2018.
This evolution was driven both by
domestic currency loans, the volume of which grew by 22.5%, and
the volume of currency recalculated in MDL by 8.3%. During 2019,
the share of the component in the national currency advanced and
reached 67.2%, amid falling interest rates (Chart 3.11).
The annual dynamics was driven by new loans granted in MDL
to individuals, whose share increased to 46.3% in 2019 (42.0%
in 2018). The volume of loans in national currency granted to
individuals increased by 35.2% compared to 2018, due to the
increase in the volume of loans for real estate and consumption.

recalculated in MDL was twice as low as in the national currency

During 2019, the growth rate of the total balance of deposits
placed with licensed banks recorded positive annual dynamics. At
the end of 2019, the annual change in the balance of deposits
increased by 0.8 percentage points compared to December 2018
and accounted for 7.9% (Chart 3.13). At the coin structure, the
balance of deposits registered at the end of the reporting year by
national currency and foreign exchange sector the same growth
rate as the total balance of deposits.
The dynamics of the balance of deposits in MDL decreased,
recording during the year 2019 values in the range of 4.5-7.8%
compared to 9.8-16.5% in 2018. The decrease in the growth rates
of the balance of deposits attracted in the national currency was
mainly due to the reduction of new term deposits attracted in MDL
and the decrease of the growth rate of the balance of sight
deposits in national currency of legal entities.
It should be
mentioned that, at the end of 2019, the share of term deposits in
the total balance of deposits in MDL was about 51.0%.
The growth rate of the balance of foreign currency deposits
expressed in the national currency was inhomogeneous and by
the end of 2019 it registered 7.9% annually, higher than at the
end of 2018 by 4.7 percentage points.
During the reporting year, the growth rate of the balance of
foreign currency deposits in US dollars decreased, which was not
homogeneous and was largely inﬂuenced by the reduction in the
balance of foreign currency term deposits of legal entities.
At the end of 2019, the total balance of deposits of legal entities
registered a growth rate of 4.6%, lower than at the end of 2018 by
2.0 percentage points. In turn, the growth rate of the balance of
deposits attracted from individuals at the end of 2019 was 9.5%,
higher than at the end of 2018 by 2.2 percentage points. It should
be noted that the share of deposits attracted from individuals in
the total balance of deposits was 67.7%.
Evolution of new attracted deposits
In 2019, the annual volume of new deposits attracted by licensed
banks had a downward dynamics compared to 2018 by 10.9%
and amounted to MDL 29,912.3 million (Chart 3.14). Analogous
to 2018, the decrease was registered both on the segment of the
national currency and on the one in foreign currency recalculated
in MDL (Chart 3.15).


46

On the national currency segment, the decrease in volumes was
more signiﬁcant compared to the foreign currency segment. The
total volume of deposits attracted in the national currency during
2019 amounted to MDL 18,719.3 million, lower than in 2018 by
13.8%. This led to a slight decrease in the share of the component
in the national currency in the total deposits attracted (62.6%
compared to 64.7% in 2018).

Interest rates applied on new deposits and loans
In the ﬁrst ﬁve months of 2019, the Executive Board of the National
Bank of Moldova maintained the base rate applied to the main
monetary policy operations at the level set at the end of 2017, of
6.5% annually. Subsequently, in June and July 2019, it successively
increased by 0.5 percentage points base rate to the level of 7.5%
annually.
In the last month of 2019, it reduced the base rate
by 2.0 percentage points up to the level of 5.5% annually. These
changes and the surplus of liquidity recorded in the banking system
throughout the reporting year inﬂuenced the average interest rates
on new term deposits and new granted loans.
Corresponding rate of new granted loans
The weighted average interest rate on new loans granted in MDL in
2019 was 8.24% annually, lower than in 2018 by 0.55 percentage
points. The weighted average interest rate on new loans granted
in MDL to legal entities was 8.76% annually, lower than in 2018
by 0.55 percentage points. The weighted average interest rate on
loans granted in MDL to individuals decreased by 0.43 percentage
points, to the level of 7.65% annually, compared to 2018.
The weighted average interest rate of new loans granted in MDL is
decreased in the ﬁrst ﬁve months until 2019 from 8.34% annually
(January 2019) to 8.08% annually (May 2019) (Chart 3.16). From
June 2019 it increased (except for July 2019) until October 2019,
when it was reached to the maximum (8.63%), and in November
and December 2019 it registered a decreasing trend again.

Over the whole of 2019, the weighted average rate on loans
granted in foreign currency was 4.42% annually, lower than in
2018 by 0.27 percentage points. The reduction in the rate was
inﬂuenced both by the interest rates on loans granted to legal
entities, whose share is predominant, and by the rates on new
loans granted in foreign currency to individuals.


Chapter 3. Monetary Policy
47
Corresponding rate of new term deposits
In 2019, being inﬂuenced by changes in the base rate applied
to the main monetary policy operations of the National Bank
of Moldova and excess liquidity in the banking system, interest
rates on new term deposits attracted by licensed banks evolved
unevenly, but with an upward trend (Chart 3.16). The weighted
average rate of term deposits attracted in the national currency
was 4.63% annually, higher than in 2018 by 0.16 percentage
points. Its evolution was largely determined by the cost of funds
attracted from individuals, whose share was 75.7%. The weighted
average interest rate on deposits attracted from individuals in the
national currency in 2019 was 4.99% annually, higher than in 2018
by 0.19 percentage points. The highest weighted average interest
rate on term deposits in the national currency was recorded in
November 2019 (5.26% annually), and the lowest in February 2019
(4.35% annually) (Chart 3.16).
The monthly evolution of the weighted average interest rates on
term deposits attracted in foreign currency was even more uneven.
In February 2019, this rate registered the historical minimum level,
dropping to 0.86% annually, and in April 2019 the maximum level
of 1.31% annually (Chart 3.15). The weighted average interest
rate on foreign currency term deposits in 2019 did not change
signiﬁcantly compared to 2018 and amounted to 1.01% annually.
Banking margin on new operations
In 2019, in the domestic currency segment, the banking margin
for new operations in MDL decreased compared to 2018 by 0.71
percentage points, registering the value of 3.61 percentage points.
In the foreign currency segment, the banking margin recorded an
annual decrease of 0.28 percentage points, standing at the level
of 3.41 percentage points (Chart 3.17).

During 2019, the average interest rates on the balance of loans
extended by licensed banks continued their downward trend (Chart
3.18). Thus, the weighted average interest rate on the balance
of loans granted in the national currency at the end of 2019 was
8.08% annually, lower than at the end of 2018 by 0.43 percentage
points.
The weighted average interest rate on the balance of loans in
foreign currency amounted to 5.07% annually, higher than at the
end of 2018 by 0.19 percentage points.
The weighted average interest rate on the balance of deposits
in MDL was 4.88% annually, higher by 0.19 percentage points
compared to the end of 2018. This dynamic was inﬂuenced by
the evolution of excessive liquidity in the banking system and the
monetary policy decisions approved in 2019. At the same time, the
weighted average interest rate on the balance of foreign currency
investment increased compared to the end of 2018 and accounted
for 1.02% annually.
At the end of 2019, the banking margin on the balance in the
national currency constituted 3.20 percentage points compared to
3.82 percentage points in 2018. The banking margin on foreign
currency balances registered 4.05 percentage points compared to
3.92 percentage points in 2018.


48
Annual Report (NBM, 2019)
3.5
Monetary market
Primary market of state securities
As a state agent responsible for the placement and administration
of state securities (SS) through the Book-Entry System, the
National Bank, in 2019, organised and carried out 121 auctions for
SS placement.
The volumes offered during the year at auctions evolved unevenly
and were adjusted according to the needs of ﬁnancing the budget
deﬁcit.
In these conditions, the Ministry of Finance tried to

maintain the maturity of the SS portfolio, keeping unchanged the
structure SS offered for placement.
The narrowing of the surplus of liquidity, partly mitigated by the
increase in interest rates on the primary market of SS, affected
investors’ interest in these investments. In total, during 2019
the ratio between the volume of bids submitted and the volume
announced for placement decreased, amounting to 1.1.
The
Ministry of Finance managed to attract from the market MDL
9,708.6 million, below the programmed value of MDL 11,225.5
million (Chart 3.19). During 2019, the evolution of interest rates
on the primary market of government securities reﬂected the
combined inﬂuences exerted by the compression of excess liquidity
in the banking sector, implicitly, by a lower interest of investors for
this type of investments and changes in the base rate of the NBM.
In the ﬁrst eight months of 2019, the average monthly rate of SS
was relatively stable, hovering around 6.0%. Between September
and November, it placed a rapidly upward trend, reﬂecting the
signiﬁcant decrease in demand for SS from investors (during
this period the ratio between the volume of bids submitted and
the volume announced for placement being subunit) and the
increasing changes in NBM rates. In December, given the decrease
in the NBM base rate and the signiﬁcant increase in demand for
these securities, this rate returned to a downward trend (Chart
3.20).
Non-bank investors remained active on the SS market due to the
attractiveness of SS interest rates, however, volumes purchased
by them during the year 2019 (MDL 1,836.8 million) increased
slightly both in absolute terms (MDL +120.9 million), as well as
the share in the total adjudicated volume (from 17.1% to 18.9%)
compared to 2018.
In the speciﬁc conditions of 2019, the investors, expressing their
aversion to risk, have opted for SS with a term of circulation of
up to one year (Chart 3.22). As a result, the share of securities
with maturity increased to 182 and 364 days to the detriment of
government bonds, whose share decreased on all maturities. Thus,
the average maturity of the issued securities decreased from 365
days to 301 days.

The interest rates on state securities recorded in 2019 values
located, mainly, in the lower part of the rate corridor established
by the NBM. The analysis of the evolution of these rates in the
maturity proﬁle shows a distancing of the interest rates related
to the securities of 182 days and 364 days compared to those
maturing in 91 days (Chart 3.23), which is explained both by the
increase by the Ministry of Finance in the management year of the
indicative volume of issues at SS with longer maturities, as well as
by the frequent adjudication of these SS in volumes that exceeded
those announced for auctions. Thus, on the primary market, the
interest rates on government securities with a maturity of up to
one year increased, but their amplitude was different depending
on the maturity term, being more accentuated on longer terms.
The average annual interest rate on state securities with a maturity
of up to one year awarded in 2019 was 6.19% versus 4.76% in
2018.
The annual weighted average nominal interest rates on SS traded
over the last two years for primary market auctions in maturity
segments are presented in the table 3.1.
In the hierarchy of returns on the ﬁnancial market, the priority was
still maintained by interest rates on loans in the economy. Marked
by a signiﬁcant increase at the end of 2018, the interest rates
related to SS rose in the middle of the corridor formed by the rates
related to loans and deposits attracted to the economy, oscillating
within it until the end of the year. The deviation between interest
rates on loans and deposits narrowed slightly during 2019 (Chart
3.24).
The volume of state securities, placed on the primary market
through auctions, in circulation as at 31 December 2019, amounted
to MDL 8,570.8 million at nominal value (Chart 3.25). At the sale-
purchase price it constituted MDL 8,253.6 million, registering an
increase of MDL 340.0 million at the end of the reported year
compared to the end of the previous year (of which MDL 90.0
million for the formation of the reserve liquidity). Of the SS in
circulation on 31 December 2019, those with a residual maturity
of up to three months accounted for 29.3%, from 3 to 6 months –
27.9%, from 6 to 12 months – 27.3%, over 1 year – 15.5%.

of MDL half a billion, amounting to MDL 586.4 million, compared
to MDL 116.1 million in the previous year. The maximum monthly

The yields of government securities with a maturity of up to 1 year
traded on the secondary market followed, in general, the trend
of the primary market of SS, being inﬂuenced only by the term of
transactions. The average annual interest rate amounted to 6.17%
(2018: 5.24%), the weighted average term was 245 days (2018:
191 days).
Regarding the structure of SS traded on the secondary market, the
participants’ interest in longer-term securities is observed (Charts
3.27 and 3.28). In this context, it should be mentioned that the
volume of government bonds (GB of 2 years and 3 years) traded
on the secondary market increased from MDL 0.5 million in 2018
to MDL 33 million in 2019. The minimum term of the treasury bill
traded was 43 days, and the most important share returned to
securities with a residual maturity of 183 to 364 days (47.5%).
Evolution of interbank market indicators in Moldovan lei
Reference interest rates applied on interbank market
The reference rate curve of CHIBOR/CHIBID (calculated on the
basis of the indicative quotations of interest rates for the
placement/attraction on the interbank market of funds in national
currency) evolved under the inﬂuence of changes in the base rate
during 2019 and the persistence of excess liquidity on the money
market (Chart 3.29).
In the ﬁrst quarter of the year, the yield curve CHIBOR was broadly
stable, sporadically slightly changing in both directions, after
which, under the inﬂuence of consecutive increases in the base
rate, it is on an upward trend that it is following until almost the
end of the year. The change in the direction of evolution of the
NBM base rate in mid-December was immediately taken over by
the CHIBOR yield curve. The interest rate CHIBOR 2W23 registered
at the end of the year constituted 7.79%.
23The reference rate calculated on the basis of the quotations of the contributing
banks for placing the funds in Moldovan lei to other banks for a period of 2 weeks.

domestic market amounted to USD 15,546.4 million, increasing by

total turnover of the domestic foreign exchange market, although
after the increases recorded in recent years, the share of the euro
decreased by 1.1 percentage points in 2019 (Charts 3.31 and
3.32). The European single currency has a much larger share in
the currency structure of the cash foreign exchange market (Table
A.6).

The European single currency has maintained its dominant position
in terms of the number of transactions. About two-thirds of foreign
exchange transactions with non-banking legal entities were against
the euro. Also, their number increased by 16.0% compared to 2018
(Chart 3.33).
A greater number of transactions in euros are recorded in volumes
up to the equivalent of USD 500 thousand. Transactions with larger
amounts were mainly made in US dollars, a signiﬁcant contribution
being the sales of US dollars to importers of energy resources
(Charts 3.34 and 3.35).
Regarding the turnover of foreign exchange operations against
MDL performed by licensed banks, its volume increased by 8.0%
to USD 13,792.6 million. This increase was mainly due to the
conversions made by banks in the intra-banking market with non-
banking legal entities and to a lesser extent, those with individuals.
Currency sales made by banks increased both to non-banking legal
entities (+8.1%) and to individuals24 (+17.1%). In the structure
of the turnover of foreign exchange operations performed by
banks with customers, only the purchases of foreign currency
from individuals decreased (-0.1%).
Signiﬁcant increases were also recorded in the interbank segment
of the domestic foreign exchange market, both between resident

The dynamics of foreign exchange assets and liabilities of
the licensed banks
During the reporting year the balance sheet foreign exchange
assets and liabilities of licensed banks25 increased, and as of
December 31, 2019 the balances amounted to USD 1,813 million
and USD 1,803.8 million, respectively, by 8.1% and 6.6% more than
on 31 December 2018. By excluding exchange rate differences26,
the increase was higher – by 9.4% and 7.8%, respectively. Despite
this dynamic, the share of foreign exchange assets and liabilities
decreased slightly in the total assets and liabilities of banks, to
34.4% and to 41.8%.
Balance sheet foreign exchange assets and liabilities remain
mostly denominated in euro. In 2019, the share of this currency
continued to strengthen, returning by 2.4 and 3.1 percentage
points more, respectively, compared to the end of 2018, up to the
proximity of 73.0% in the structure of both indicators (Charts 3.36
and 3.38).
In the structure of assets by elements, there were no major
changes compared to 2018, except for the fact that the required
reserves of banks increased by 25.6%, to USD 260.8 million (Chart
3.37). This increase was due to the NBM’s decision to increase,
in the second half of the year, the norm of required foreign
exchange reserves by 3 percentage points, given that the licensed
banks continued to have an excessive level of foreign currency
liquidity in order to ensure ﬂuency in making current settlements
by customers.
At the end of the reporting year, the balance of foreign currency
cash of banks – 7.3%27 higher than at the end of 2018 – became
comparable to that of foreign currency loans. Thus, the balance
of banks’ foreign currency cash amounted to USD 717.4 million
(Tables A.8 and A.9) and accounted for 39.5% of total balance
sheet foreign exchange assets, reaching higher weights during the
year (49.7%).
25Both indicators exclude the adjustment position and include assets and liabilities
attached to foreign exchange rates.
26Arising from the recalculation of currencies in US dollars in which foreign
exchange assets and liabilities are denominated.
278.5% by excluding exchange rate differences.


54
Annual Report (NBM, 2019)
Regarding the foreign exchange structure of local banks’ cash,
on December 31, 2019 the majority share went to the European
single currency – 70.4%, followed by the US dollar – 26.7%, the
Russian ruble – 1.2% and other currencies – 1.7%.
During 2019, as a whole, the foreign currency lending activity
of licensed banks continued to be relatively low, but evolved
differently depending on the loan currency. Thus, the loan portfolio
granted in US dollars (including those granted in MDL attached at
the exchange rate of the MDL against the US dollar) decreased by
19.1%, while the loan portfolio granted in euro (including those
granted in MDL attached to the exchange rate against the euro)
increased by 9.0%.

the end of 2018, the balance of foreign currency loans granted
(including those attached to the foreign exchange rate) increased
insigniﬁcantly, by 0.4%, to USD 857.0 million (of which USD 96.9
million were attached to the foreign exchange rate). The balance
of foreign currency loans granted did not exceed 50.4% in the
structure of balance sheet foreign exchange assets, and at the end
of the management year it amounted to 47.4% – by 3.7 percentage
points less than at the end of 2018.
With regard to the liabilities part, its increase was generated by
the attraction by banks of funds in euro (+11.2%).
In the structure of the balance sheet foreign exchange liabilities
of the licensed banks, 90.0% returned to the traditional ones:
term and sight deposits28 of non-bank customers (hereinafter
– foreign currency accounts).
At the end of 2019, the foreign
currency balance sheet accounts amounted to USD 1,622.6 million,
increasing by 6.8%, and by excluding exchange rate differences
– by 8.0% compared to the end of 2018 (Chart 3.39, Tables A.10
and A.11).
In 2019, bank customers, both individuals and legal entities,
preferred to increase their investments in sight accounts, mainly in
euros to the detriment of term deposits. Respectively, the balance
of fassets held on sight in customers’ accounts increased by 18.5%
to USD 948.9 million, and the balance of term assets decreased
by 6.3% to USD 673.7 million.
However, foreign currency term deposits attracted from non-bank
customers of banks, mostly individuals, covered in signiﬁcant
proportions, 88.3%, of loans granted in foreign currency by
licensed banks (excluding those attached to the foreign exchange
rate).
As of 31 December 2019 compared to 31 December 2018, banks’
foreign currency liabilities to individuals remained virtually
unchanged in share, amounting to 63.0% of total balance sheet
foreign exchange liabilities. Their balance increased by 6.2% to
USD 1,136 million. The balance of foreign exchange accounts of
non-banking legal entities increased by 8.1% to USD 487.0 million
and amounted to 27.0% of the total balance sheet foreign
exchange liabilities of licensed banks.
In 2019, the level of attraction by banks of foreign currency loans
remains to be reduced, especially of the external ones, the loan
repayments by the licensed banks exceeding the withdrawals.
28Current accounts and various deposits.

10.0% (excluding exchange rate inﬂuence) to USD 128.9 million.
At the end of the reference year, 8.1% of the balance sheet foreign
exchange liabilities of licensed banks were in relation to non-
residents compared to 9.8% as of 31 December 2018.

Evolution of nominal and real effective exchange rate
In 2019, the national currency depreciated against the US dollar
by 0.4% according to the values at the end of the year and by
4.6% in average values, and against the European single currency
it appreciated by 1.3% according to the values at the end of the
year and by 0.9% in average values (Chart 3.40).
As for the dynamics against the US dollar of the currencies of the
main trading partners, with notable shares in the foreign trade of
the
Republic
of
Moldova,
compared
to
the
Moldovan
leu,
according to the situation at the end of 2019, some partners
’currencies depreciated to a greater extent, a development
completely attenuated by the appreciation of other partners’
currencies (Chart 3.41).
In this sense,
the Moldovan leu
depreciated in nominal terms, by 0.3%, compared to the basket of
the currencies of the main trading partners weighted at their
shares in the foreign trade of the Republic of Moldova (NEER).
Despite this fact, in real terms, the Moldovan leu appreciated by
3.7% against the basket (REER), the dynamics being exclusively
caused by the positive inﬂation differential between the Republic
of Moldova and most of the partner countries included in the
basket (Charts 3.42 and 3.43).
The evolution of the foreign exchange market in 2019 was
inﬂuenced by several factors. The growth of trade with foreign
countries has moderated, amid slowing global economic growth,
respectively in the main trading partner countries of the Republic
of Moldova.
Thus, the export and import of goods increased
insigniﬁcantly, by 2.7% and 1.4%, and consequently the trade
balance of goods remained virtually unchanged compared to
2018. The rate of increase in transfers from abroad in favor of
individuals has also moderated to 2.8%, in this case due to the
decrease of those in the Russian Federation. The ﬂow of foreign
direct investment in the national economy increased, highlighting
the inﬂows in favor of a single economic agent. At the same time,
net dividend payments in favor of foreign founders have reached
a historical maximum level.


56
Annual Report (NBM, 2019)
In the reporting year, the dynamics of the Moldovan leu exchange
rate against the US dollar can be divided into two periods. In the
ﬁrst half of the year, the exchange rate followed a depreciation
trend, determined by a shortage of currency in the market, and
in the second half, with the formation of a foreign exchange
surplus, there was a correction in the exchange rate, it reversing
its trajectory towards appreciation.

exchange rate,

operators

subjective

to the electoral periods.

foreign currency was generated by the seasonal needs of
importers of energy resources, the increase in foreign exchange
sales to them being conditioned by the trend of temporary
increase in fuel prices in foreign markets. With a higher demand
for foreign currency came some companies with foreign capital
that distributed a part of the ﬁnancial result to foreign investors.
In addition, the supply of foreign currency from individuals was
reduced, as a result of the negative evolution of transfers from
abroad in their favor, followed in the ﬁrst half of the year.
In this sense, in the ﬁrst half of 2019, the net demand for foreign
currency from economic operators increased by 14.9%, that of
importers of energy resources inﬂuencing by 1/4 the increase in
net demand compared to the ﬁrst half of 2018. The net supply of
currency from individuals decreased by 2.5%. Respectively, there
was a shortage of foreign exchange on the foreign exchange
market, which was covered by the NBM through net foreign
exchange sales of about USD 112 million. In addition, the NBM
has carried out bilateral euro procurement operations with some
banks in the amount of EUR 47.4 million (the equivalent of USD
53.8 million) in connection with some larger foreign investment
projects.
Unlike the developments recorded in the ﬁrst half of the year, in
the second half of 2019, the net demand for foreign currency from
economic operators decreased by 1.5%, that from importers of
energy resources – by 12.6% compared to the second half of 2018.
At the same time, in the second half of 2019, the disaggregated
indicator shows a higher increase in supply compared to the
demand for foreign currency from economic operators, highlighting
foreign currency purchases made by banks from exporters of
cereals and oilseeds and from new retail investors. The net supply
of foreign currency from individuals moderated its decrease to
1.1% compared to the second half of 2018 and increased compared
to the previous half. Under these conditions, a foreign exchange
surplus was formed on the foreign exchange market, which created
premises for the NBM’s intervention of net purchase of foreign
currency of about USD 59 million.
Over the year as a whole, the net demand for foreign currency
from economic operators amounted to about USD 2,179 million,
increasing by 5.3%, and the net supply of foreign currency from
individuals amounted to about USD 2,089 million and decreased
by 1.8%. The net supply of foreign currency from individuals was
mainly in euros, at the same time there was an increase in the net
supply in other currencies, mostly in pounds sterling (Chart 3.45).
Consequently, the degree of coverage of net demand through
the net supply of foreign currency decreased to 95.9% in 2019

from 102.8% in 2018 (Chart 3.44), and to cover this gap the NBM
intervened through net sales of USD 5.0 million.

Ofﬁcial reserve assets
According to the situation at the end of the year, the ofﬁcial
reserve assets amounted to USD 3,059.6 million, increasing by
2.2% compared to 2018, as a result of ﬁnancial assistance from
the main development partners of the Republic of Moldova. In this
regard:
• The IMF allocated the ﬁfth tranche of USD 46.0 million
(equivalent to SDR 33.5 million), of which the NBM received
SDR 13.5 million (EFF29 – SDR 9.0 million, ECF – SDR 4.5
million), and the rest of the amount went to the Ministry of
Finance for budget support (EFF – SDR 13.4 million, ECF –
SDR 6.7 million);
• The European Commission has provided, on behalf of the EU,
funds for budget support, mostly grants in the amount of
USD 92.8 million (equivalent to EUR 83.6 million), of which
USD 33.2 million (equivalent to EUR 30 million) was the ﬁrst
tranche of macro-ﬁnancial assistance;
• The
European
Investment
Bank
has
allocated
loans
amounting to USD 51.9 million (equivalent to EUR 46.1
million) for road rehabilitation and support for the "Moldova’s
Orchard" project;
• The World Bank, through its IDA and IBRD institutions, has
provided a majority of USD 15.6 million in loans to support the
projects: Improving Competitiveness II, Rehabilitating Local
Roads, Education Reform, Modernizing Government Services,
etc.;
• IFAD continued to fund the IFAD VI and VII Inclusive Rural
Economic and Climate Resilience Program by allocating USD
6.3 million (57% as credit, the remaining part as grant);
• The Council of Europe Development Bank disbursed USD
2.1 million (equivalent to EUR 1.9 million) to ﬁnance the
construction of Social Housing II and Ambulance Insurance,
which beneﬁted the Ministry of Health, Labour and Social
Protection.
At the end of 2019, the ofﬁcial reserve assets covered in sufﬁcient
proportions the imports, constituting 5.6 months of actual import
of goods and services.
Managing foreign exchange reserves
According to Article 5, 16 and 53 of the Law on the National Bank
of Moldova, the NBM maintains and manages the foreign exchange
reserves of the state, performs foreign exchange operations using
foreign exchange reserves and maintains them at an appropriate
level for the conduct of the monetary and foreign exchange policy
of the state. The role of foreign exchange reserves is to ensure
29EFF – Extended Fund Facility, ECF – Extended Credit Facility.


58
Annual Report (NBM, 2019)
the credibility and ﬁnancial stability of the country, to honor
the payments of the state’s external liabilities and to deal with
unpredictable exogenous shocks.
In the process of managing foreign exchange reserves, the NBM
ensures a high degree of security and a necessary level of
liquidity of investments.
NBM’s investment policy is prudent,
aiming at optimizing proﬁtability, provided that liquidity and
security of investments are ensured. An essential element of the
process of managing foreign exchange reserves is investment risk
management, which is achieved by imposing constraints and
limits on investment.
Table A.13 lists the main risks associated with the management
of foreign exchange reserves and the methods applied to reduce
them.
Starting with July 2013, the Strategic Asset Allocation (SAA) is
being implemented in the framework of the World Bank. Strategic
Asset Allocation involves a long-term asset management vision
focused on achieving optimal proﬁtability and risk. In the context
of the SAA, international reserves are divided into three tranches
(current, liquidity and investment) in order to ensure a more
efﬁcient achievement of the set objective.
In 2019, the world economy recorded the slowest growth rate
(2.9%) since the global ﬁnancial crisis a decade ago, despite all
monetary and ﬁscal measures taken. The US-China trade war, the
uncertainty about the evolution of Brexit, the geopolitical and
social tensions, climate change – the main cause of natural
disasters, have contributed to the slowdown in the annual pace of
economic growth. Rising trade barriers and associated uncertainty
have had a negative impact on the business environment and
global development prospects.
In the case of the advanced
economies
and
China,
these
developments
have
further
accentuated the cyclical and structural slowdowns already under
way.
The increase in uncertainty associated with the economic
environment has led to a precarious attitude of the business
environment towards long-term expenditures, and purchases of
machinery and equipment have decreased.
In response to
declining
demand
for
durable
goods,
industrial
production
declined, thereby affecting global trade.
These factors have
signiﬁcantly contributed to the slowdown in global economic
growth.
These developments have led to appropriate reactions from central
banks.
During 2019, several central banks, including the US
Federal Reserve System, the European Central Bank and emerging
market banks, cut the interest rates, while the ECB also resumed
asset purchases. These policies aimed to avoid an even more
pronounced slowdown. Reductions in global central bank rates
dominated the capital markets, especially in the second half of the
year, with the scale of these monetary incentives being the widest
in this decade.
Towards the end of 2019, the world economy remains unstable
and severely affected by uncertainties, including that of the
coronavirus pandemic which demonstrates once again how any
boom can be threatened by unforeseen events.


Chapter 3. Monetary Policy
59
The
process
of
managing
foreign
exchange
reserves
is
conditioned by the evolution of the world economy, the decisions
of the world’s major central banks, especially the US monetary
authorities, the euro area and the United Kingdom, taking into
account the importance of their currencies in the structure of
ofﬁcial reserve assets.
The monetary policy objective of the SRF Federal Open Market
Committee is to keep inﬂation at 2% and to achieve a maximum
level of employment.
After, during 2018, the economic activity, as well as the labour
market indicators registered constant improvements, and the base
rate was increased 4 times by 25 basis points, being at the end of
the year in the corridor of 2.25-2.50%, in 2019 a further promotion
of the restrictive monetary policy was anticipated. Subsequent
developments, however, were contrary to expectations. During
March 2019, for the ﬁrst time after 2008, we witnessed a reversal
of the yield curve of securities issued by the US Treasury, the
rates on the 10-year segment being lower than the 3-month ones.
At the meeting of the Federal Open Market Committee of SRF on
March 19-20, 2019, the evolution of macroeconomic indicators was
presented and their forecast was revised, highlighting a decrease
in the growth rate of economic activity compared to the fourth
quarter of 2018. In addition, it is understood that the monetary
policy rate will most likely remain unchanged during 2019. Thus,
the reaction of investors associated with the uncertainty and
estimates of a likely recession in the US over 1-2 years has caused
an abnormal change in the yield curve, in particular in segments
2, 3 and 5 years, which was also maintained during the second
quarter, amid the expectations of a potential decline in the base
rate, the worsening of the US-China trade dispute and statistical
data related to the US labour market.
On 31 July 2019, SRF decreased the base rate by 25 basis points,
targeting the range of 2.0-2.25%, this being the ﬁrst rate decrease
in the last 11 years. At the same time, the members of the Federal
Open Market Committee gave assurances that there will be no
successive reductions in the rate, the decision being classiﬁed as
a mid-cycle adjustment or a recalibration in response to changing
market conditions. Despite those mentioned, on 18 September
2019, the rate was again reduced by 25 basis points, to the level
of 1.75-2.0%. Although the SRF did not forecast further rate cuts
in either 2019 or 2020, the members of the committee had divided
views on further actions (5 members felt that the rate should be
maintained at the previous level of 2.0-2.25%, another 5 members
supported the reduction of the rate and the maintenance of the
level until the end of 2019, while 7 members opted for at least one
more decrease of the rate in 2019).
Finally, on 30 October 2019, the third rate decrease took place,
the target range being 1.50-1.75%. SRF argued its decision as an
appropriate action to support the expansion, saying that, most
likely, there will be a "break" in reducing the rate.
At the last meeting of the Federal Open Market Committee of SRF
in 2019, it was decided to keep the reﬁnancing rate in the corridor
of 1.50-1.75%. According to the forecasts of 13 out of 17 SRF
ofﬁcials, the rates were to remain at the same level during 2020
and record a single increase in 2021.


60
Annual Report (NBM, 2019)
The basic objective of the European Central Bank is to maintain
price stability, at a lower inﬂation rate but close to 2% in the
medium term. In order to achieve this objective, the ECB uses
the interest rates as an instrument of monetary policy and, after
the crisis, other unconventional measures, the effect of which
is felt on ﬁnancing conditions in the economy.
By managing
ﬁnancing conditions, the ECB can inﬂuence the overall level of
economic activity and ensure that the inﬂation targeting target
is met. During 2019, the ECB maintained the interest rate on
the main reﬁnancing operations at 0.0% and the interest rate on
overnight lending facilities at 0.25%. At the same time, at the
monetary policy meeting of 11-12 September 2019, the decision
was taken to reduce the interest rate on overnight deposit facilities
by 10 basis points from (-0.4)% to (-0.5)%. At the same meeting,
it was decided to resume asset purchases amounting to EUR
20 billion starting in November 2019, for a period sufﬁcient to
consolidate the accommodative impact of monetary policy rates
and to close them shortly before the rise in key ECB rates. Also,
principal payments on overdue securities purchased under the
asset purchase program will continue to be reinvested for an
extended period of time to maintain favorable liquidity conditions.
These decisions were taken as a result of the unfavorable dynamics
of the euro area’s economic growth, mainly due to developments
in the external sector, in particular international trade. During
2019, successive downward revisions of the inﬂation outlook were
observed, thus, the set of measures aimed to anchor and stabilize
inﬂation around a level close to, but lower than 2%.
Despite all the actions taken, the annual inﬂation rate in the euro
area at the end of 2019 stood at 1.2%.
The central monetary policy objective of the Central Bank of
England is also to target inﬂation at 2.0% in a way that would
support economic growth and the employment rate. Following the
referendum of 23 June 2016, when 51.9% of the citizens agreed to
leave the European Union, the Brexit process was due to end on
29 March 2019.
However, this was delayed, as there was no
consensus on the withdrawal agreement, as the parties were
interested in a more amicable exit of the United Kingdom from the
European Union,
without affecting trade relations and the
population too much. The deadline for signing the agreement was
initially extended twice, after which the Parliament rejected the
agreement negotiated by the former Prime Minister of the United
Kingdom. The third failure to vote in support of the amended
agreement resulted in the resignation of the Prime Minister.
Subsequently, the new Prime Minister needed a further extension
of the Brexit deadline until 31 January 2020 in order to
successfully complete the process. The result of the early general
elections of 12 December 2019 and the considerable majority of
Conservatives in the Parliament facilitated the adoption of Brexit
legislation.
Under these conditions, the economic growth of Great Britain

At the same time,
uncertainty about Brexit has affected the dynamics of the pound

of 1.33, at the end of the year the pound sterling registering
an appreciation of about 4.0% compared to the end of 2018

• maintaining, throughout the year, at a level of GBP 435.0

Ofﬁcial reserve assets, as of 31 December 2019 compared to 31
December 2018, increased by 2.2%, from USD 2,995.2 million to
USD 3,059.6 million.
In the process of managing foreign exchange reserves, the National
Bank invests in safe instruments used by other central banks:
investments in correspondent accounts (usually other central
banks), term placements and securities, which are classiﬁed as
supranational securities (issued by supranational institutions),
governmental (issued by the US government, governments of
European Union member states and other governmental issuers)
and non-governmental (issued by agencies).
Some of the foreign exchange reserves are managed externally
by the World Bank. In accordance with the Investment Consulting
and Management Agreement between the International Bank for
Reconstruction and Development (IBRD) and the National Bank
of Moldova of December 8, 2010 (extended by the DEB no. 168
of December 30, 2015), IBRD became the NBM’s consultant and
agent for the administration of a part of the foreign assets, limited
to 20% of the foreign exchange reserves. As of 31 December
2019, the share of externally managed assets was 6.7% of foreign
exchange reserves.
According to the situation as of 31 December 2019, 55.2% of the
reserves are invested in securities, 20.2% are term placements,
and 24.6% – are kept in cash and funds in corresponding accounts
(Chart 3.48).
Return on investment depends on market conditions, exchange
rate ﬂuctuations and returns on investment instruments.
The
evolution of the global economy and the development prospects
of the ﬁnancial markets have inﬂuenced the investment decisions
and the foreign exchange composition of the reserves. In this
context, the majority share (according to year-end values) in the
structure of foreign exchange reserves was held by the USD –
66.4% (Chart 3.49).
At the same time, the foreign exchange composition of the
reserves may deviate within the permissible deviation limits of
±10 percentage points from the normative foreign currency
structure (Chart 3.49), approved by the Decision of the Executive
Board no. 309 of 20 December 2018, in force since 1 January
2019.


62
Annual Report (NBM, 2019)
Starting from the evolution of the global economy in 2019,
especially of the USA, EU and UK, the conjuncture of the ﬁnancial
markets and the evolution of exchange rates against the US dollar
(reserve
reporting
currency),
the
management
of
foreign
exchange reserves generated a proﬁt of USD 50.8 million, at an
average proﬁtability rate of 1.71%.


63
Chapter 4
Banking supervision
4.1
Evolution of the banking sector
Core indicators of the banking system
On 31 December 2019, there were 11 banks licensed by the
National Bank of Moldova operating in the Republic of Moldova,
including 5 subsidiaries of foreign banks and ﬁnancial groups.
The total number of subdivisions of licensed banks in the Republic
of Moldova at the end of 2019 was 715, of which 295 subsidiaries
and 420 agencies.
There were 7,895 people working in the banking sector.
On
average, each employee in the banking sector had to manage
assets amounting to MDL 11.5 million, increasing by MDL 0.9
million (8.1%) compared to the end of 2018.
At the end of 2019, the ﬁnancial situation of the banking sector was
good enough. Banks were sufﬁciently capitalized, their own funds
amounted to MDL 11,500.5 million, increasing by MDL 612.2 million
(5.6%) compared to the previous year, and the total equity ratio
remains at a high level, registering the value of 25.3%, decreasing
by 1.2 percentage points compared to 2018, the mandatory rules
being observed by all banks.
The dynamics of the main ﬁnancial indicators are shown in the
chart 4.1.
As of 31 December 2019, the proﬁt for the banking sector
amounted to MDL 2,257.5 million, increasing compared to the
previous year by MDL 805.9 million (55.5%). For 2019, the return
on assets30 and return on capital31 of licensed banks recorded
values of 2.5% and 14.6%, respectively, increasing compared to
the previous year by 0.8 percentage points and by 4.3 percentage

Own funds
At the end of 2019, the own funds of the banking sector amounted
to MDL 11,500.5 million, registering an increase of MDL 612.2
million (5.6%) during 2019.
The increase of own funds was determined by the reﬂection by
the banks of the eligible proﬁts after the holding of the general
meetings of the shareholders and after obtaining the NBM’s
permission regarding the inclusion in the own funds of the
obtained proﬁts.
At the same time, the positive difference
between prudential reductions and IFRS reductions decreased by
MDL 31.5 million and other reserves by MDL 63.0 million. At the
same time, other intangible assets increased by MDL 15.6 million.
Also, a bank distributed capital in the form of dividends in the
total amount of MDL 221.0 million.
The structure of total own funds in 10 banks consists entirely of
core tier 1 own funds (total per sector – MDL 11,374.2 million),
and a bank (BC "ProCredit Bank" S.A.) also has tier 2 own funds
(subordinated loans in the amount of MDL 126.3 million).
At the end of the year, the rate of total own funds on the banking
sector registered the value of 25.3%, decreasing by 1.2 percentage
points compared to the end of the previous year. This indicator is
followed by all banks (limit for each bank ≥10%).

(7.7%). Another source for ﬁnancing assets was capital, which
increased by MDL 2,175.7 million (15.3%) as a result of obtaining
proﬁt during the reference period.

percentage points from 43.2% to 43.7% (Chart 4.8).

It should be noted that the limits of the dominant position on

share of the bank’s total assets in relation to the total assets on

Following the analysis of the concentration of the banking sector
of the Republic of Moldova, the Herﬁndahl-Hirschmann index was
calculated (the value of which does not exceed 10,000), based on
the share of the bank’s assets in total assets in the banking sector.
This index is calculated as the sum of the squares of the market
shares of all banks in the sector. In the international practice there
is used the following division:
• HHI below 100 indicates a perfectly competitive market;
• HHI between 100 and 1,500 indicates a non-concentrated
market;
• HHI
between
1,500
and
2,500
indicates
a
moderate
concentration;
• HHI above 2,500 indicates a high concentration.
Thus, following the calculations performed, obtaining HHI=1,750.2,
we conclude that the banking sector of the Republic of Moldova
has a moderate concentration.

During 2019, non-performing loans (substandard, doubtful and
compromised) in absolute value decreased by MDL 1,017.0
million (22.9%), amounting to MDL 3,428.3 million, as a result of
derecognition of non-performing loans and repayment of loans
from direct payments. Respectively, the share of non-performing
loans in total loans decreased by 4.0 percentage points compared
to the end of 2018, amounting to 8.5% on 31 December 2019,
and the share of net non-performing loans32 in own funds – by 3.0
percentage points, constituting 10.0% on 31 December 2019.
The share of reductions calculated for loan losses in total loans
amounted to 8.0%, decreasing compared to the end of 2018 by 2.9
percentage points, mainly due to the improvement during 2019 of
the quality of the loan portfolio, as well as of its growth. It should be
noted that the reductions calculated on all assets and contingent
liabilities as of 31 December 2019 amounted to MDL 4,766.9
million, and the reductions for impairment losses constituted MDL
2,903.4 million, the difference recorded – MDL 1,863.5 million.
The share of impairment losses on loans (IFRS reductions) in the
calculated loan reductions (prudential reductions) on the banking
sector was 63.5%, ranging from 19.7% to 92.7%.
Banks’ exposures to afﬁliates show insigniﬁcant shares in total
loans – 2.5% (average in the banking sector). The indicator the
ratio between exposures to afﬁliates and eligible capital (the limit
for each bank ≤20.0% of eligible capital) as of 31 December 2019
was observed by all banks (average in the banking sector – 8.8%).
On 31 December 2019, the indicator of the share of the ten
largest net debts in the total loan portfolio and contingent liabilities
(maximum limit for each bank – 30% of total net loans) was met
by all banks (average in the banking sector – 21.2%).
At the same time, only one bank registered the exceeding of the
above-mentioned indicator, which is not considered a violation if
the bank maintains an additional own funds requirement regarding
the respective surplus.
The indicator of the total amount of the bank’s net exposures
in Moldovan lei attached to the foreign exchange rate against
individuals, including those engaged in entrepreneurial activity
or other type of activity (the limit for each bank ≤30% of OF)
was observed by all banks. The sector average of the nominated
indicator was 2.9%.

non-interest income by 12.6% or MDL 330.4 million, following the
increase in fees and commissions income,
exchange rate
differences etc. Signiﬁcant shares in non-interest income occupy

For 2019, the return on assets33 and return on capital34 of
licensed banks recorded values of 2.5% and correspondingly
14.6%, increasing by 0.8 percentage points and 4.3 percentage
points compared to the end of the previous year.
The net interest margin35 amounted to 4.4% on 31 December
2019, being in insigniﬁcant increase by 0.1 percentage points
compared to the end of the previous year.
The dynamics of the net interest margin, return on assets and
capital of the banking sector of the Republic of Moldova for the
period 31 December 2018 - 31 December 2019 is shown in chart
4.15.
The absolute value of interest-earning assets increased by MDL
7,399.7 million or 10.8%, amounting to MDL 75,792.6 million at the
end of the year. The signiﬁcant share of interest-earning assets in
the total assets of the banking sector, which accounted for 83.7%,
indicates the ability of banks to generate income in the future.
Liquidity and compliance with legal requirements
The liabilities of banks as of 31 December 2019 amounted to MDL
74,190.1 million,
increasing by MDL 5,347.1 million (7.8%)
compared to 31 December 2018.
The largest share in total
liabilities is held by ﬁnancial liabilities valued at amortized cost
(customer deposits, other loans, subordinated debts) – 97.6%
(MDL 72,374.6 million), these increasing in absolute value by MDL
4,986.1 million (7.4%) compared to the end of the previous year.
Customer deposits amounted to MDL 68,449.7 million or 92.3% of
total liabilities, the absolute value of customer deposits increasing
compared to 31 December 2018 by MDL 4,872.3 million (7.7%).
Also, other liabilities were increased (transit and clearing amounts,
operations with payment cards, settlements with bank employees,
other
natural
and
legal
persons,
budget

4.2
The NMB supervisory activity
During 2019, the National Bank of Moldova (NBM) maintained
efforts to ensure sound corporate governance,
shareholder
transparency, attract potential investors and reduce the risks to
which banks are exposed, in order to maintain a stable banking
sector.
The main areas on which the banking supervision process was
focused in 2019, according to the set priorities, were: the quality
and transparency of the shareholders; banks’ internal governance,
risk management and internal capital adequacy and assessment
processs (ICAAP); lending activity, credit risk and exposures to


72
Annual Report (NBM, 2019)
afﬁliates; the risk associated with information and communication
technologies (ICT); combating money laundering and terrorist
ﬁnancing.
The supervisory activities were carried out through both on-site
and off-site inspections, using all the supervisory tools available to
the National Bank.
Starting with 2019, the NBM supervises banks based on the SREP
supervisory review and evaluation process, this being the main
prudential supervision instrument of the NBM. Within the SREP
process, the following aspects are evaluated: the analysis of the
business model; evaluation of the bank’s management framework
and internal control mechanisms; capital risk assessment; liquidity
risk assessment; assessing the adequacy of the bank’s own funds;
assessing the adequacy of the bank’s liquidity resources; general
evaluation of SREP. Following the evaluation of the SREP, the NBM
may apply supervisory measures as well as early intervention
measures, as appropriate.
Off-site inspections
The National Bank performs the off-site supervision based on the
annual prudential supervisory program. For 2019, the prudential
supervisory program included various current actions (monitoring
of key indicators, analysis of ﬁnancial reports submitted by banks,
external audit reports, monitoring of remedial plans of banks
submitted as a result of on-site inspections), and actions in order
to prepare the SREP report (evaluation of the business model,
analysis of the reports prepared by the audit subdivisions, including
compliance, risks, evaluation of recovery plans etc.).
In 2019, loan risk remained one of the important areas of
supervisory priorities. The NBM examined, on a daily basis, ex
ofﬁcio the loan portfolio of each bank on the basis of the Credit
Risk Register in order to supervise and identify imprudent
practices.
In case of classiﬁcation of loans contrary to the
regulations in force, the NBM requested the removal of the
detected violations.
As a result of the monitoring and transparency actions carried
out by the NBM, in 2019 there have been applied some sanctions
to some shareholders of B.C. "ENERGBANK" S.A. and "Banca de
Finant,e s, i Comert," S.A. which did not comply with the requirements
of the relevant legislation. Thus, it was found the concerted activity
of some groups of shareholders who cumulatively owned qualiﬁed
holdings in the capital of banks without the prior approval of
the National Bank, with the legal suspension of the exercise of
certain rights of the respective shareholders; subsequently, there
were suspended some rights of qualiﬁed shareholders in the share
capital of B.C. "ENERGBANK" S.A., for their non-compliance with
the requirements on adequacy and ﬁnancial soundness stipulated
in the Law on Banks’ Activity and the normative acts in force.
At the same time, during 2019, major changes occurred in the
shareholding structure of three systemic banks:
• on 22 January 2019, the Executive Board of the NBM granted
prior approval for the acquisition by "Doverie-Invest" S.A.
(Bulgaria) of the qualiﬁed holding in the share capital of


Chapter 4. Banking supervision
73
BC "Moldindconbank" S.A. in the amount of 63.89%.
In
February 2019, the said package of shares was purchased
by the Public Property Agency.
Subsequently, during the
auction, the package of shares in the amount of 63.89% of
the share capital of BC "Moldindconbank" S.A. was sold to
"Doverie-Invest" S.A. During 2019, the shareholder "Doverie-
Invest" S.A. increased his qualiﬁed holding to 77.63%, as a
result of the completion of the offer to take over the bank’s
shares (13.73%) and the acquisition of holdings from others
shareholders of the bank (0.01%);
• on 28 February 2019, the Executive Board of the NBM issued
the prior approval for the acquisition of the qualiﬁed holding
in the amount of 48.37% of the share capital of BC "MOLDOVA-
AGROINDBANK" S.A. by the group of persons consisting of
"HEIM PARTNERS LIMITED" and another 5 shareholders of the
bank based on the usufruct agreement concluded. Thus the
consortium of investors formed by EBRD, Invalda INVL and
Horizon Capital through "HEIM PARTNERS LIMITED" controls
48.37% of the share capital of BC "MOLDOVA-AGROINDBANK"
S.A.;
• on 10 July 2019, the Executive Board of the NBM approved the
acquisition by OTP Bank Nyrt., Hungary, of the share control
package (more than 50%) of the share capital of Mobiasbanca
– OTP Group S.A. Thus, in the context of the registration of
the ownership right of the new shareholder OTP BANK Nyrt,
the ofﬁcial name of the bank was changed to Mobiasbanca –
OTP Group S.A., registered the bank’s statute in new wording,
registered the new composition of the Bank’s Board, as well
as the chairman of the Steering Committee – CEO.
The National Bank will continue to act in accordance with current
legislation
and
international
best
practices,
applying
all
instruments assigned by law and statute, in order to increase the
level of transparency of the ownership structure and ensure
prudent and sound management of licensed banks.
On-site inspections
During 2019, there were performed 7 on-site inspections at the
licensed banks, out of which 2 complex inspections and 5 thematic
inspections. During 2019, there were applied 6 sanctions, of which:
warnings against 4 banks and against 2 people with key positions.
The decisions on the application of sanctions and prescriptions
were taken as a result of the ﬁnding of infringements and
deﬁciencies, mainly related to the following:
• the lack of clear procedures regarding the knowledge of the
direct, indirect owners of the bank’s shareholders, of the
actual beneﬁciaries, including the knowledge by the bank of
their possible concerted activities;
• the deﬁciencies in the bank’s management framework
(annual non-review of the bank’s internal documents, failure
to audit all outsourced activities/ operations, presentation to
the NBM of reports with some erroneous data);
• the existence of other violations and shortcomings in the
areas: the reserve requirements regime and the information
security management system.


74
Annual Report (NBM, 2019)
At the same time, we mention that in 2019, there were performed
186 inspections at the foreign exchange units, out of which 172
planned inspections and 14 unexpected inspections, including 1
planned control at the foreign exchange points of the hotels. As a
result of the violations and deﬁciencies found during the on-site
inspections carried out at the foreign exchange units, these have
been applied 33 sanctions (4 decisions to suspend the activity, 1
decision to withdraw the license, 5 decisions to apply the ﬁne, 22
sanctions by issuing written warnings).
Decisions on the application of sanctions to foreign exchange units
(other than banks) were taken following the ﬁnding of violations
and deﬁciencies, the basis being:
• reporting with errors/ non-reporting/ modiﬁcation of the
information regarding the buying/ selling rates of the main
currencies established by the exchange ofﬁces;
• violations regarding the exchange rate disposition and
internal procedures on how to change the exchange rate;
• violations related to the development of USV activity without
notifying the NBM.
Intensive supervision and early intervention
On 4 April 2019, the Executive Board of the National Bank of
Moldova lifted the intensive supervision regime of BC "MOLDOVA-
AGROINDBANK" S.A. and revoked the prescribed measures during
the special supervision.
The decision was made after it was
found that the bank ensures the transparency of the shareholding
structure, following the acquisition of 41.09% of the bank’s share
capital by "HEIM PARTNERS LIMITED", consisting of an international
consortium of investors: EBRD, Invalda INVL, Horizon Capital. At
the same time, from 21 March 2019, the Board of Directors of BC
"MOLDOVA-AGROINDBANK" S.A. started exercising its mandate, in
the composition elected by the decision of the Ordinary Annual
General Meeting of the bank’s shareholders from 22 November
2018. On 14 June 2019, the NBM approved the 7th member of the
Bank’s Board, its composition becoming complete.
Regarding BC "MOLDINDCONBANK" S.A.,
in 2019 the bank
remained under the early intervention regime, as a result of the
ﬁnding of the concerted activity of a group of persons that
acquired and held a substantial share in the bank’s share capital
in the amount of 63.89%, without the prior written permission of
the NBM, thus violating the provisions of the Law on Financial
Institutions.
The term of ofﬁce of the bank’s temporary
administrators was extended twice during the reference year.
On 18 January 2019, the Executive Board of the National Bank
of Moldova decided to extend by three months the term of sale
of the newly issued shares by BC "Moldindconbank" S.A., as a
single package amounting to 63.89% of the share capital of the
bank. Subsequently, on 22 January 2019, there was approved the
decision of the Executive Board of the NBM, regarding the granting
of the prior permission for the acquisition by the potential direct
purchaser "Doverie-Invest" S.A. (Bulgaria) of qualiﬁed holding in a

size that reaches and exceeds the level of 50% in the share capital
of the bank.

of

by

"Moldindconbank" S.A.,

on
"Banca
de

s, i

S.A.
and
BC
"ENERGBANK" S.A.
Thus, regarding "Banca de Finant,e s, i Comert," S.A., the National
Bank found that a group of persons acts in concert with the bank,
which acquired and owns a qualiﬁed holding (substantial share)
in the share capital of the bank in the amount of 36.15%, without
the prior written permission of the National Bank, violating the
provisions of the Law on Banks’ Activity.
As a result, the law
suspended the exercise of certain rights of these shareholders
(voting rights, convening and holding the general meeting of
shareholders, to put issues on the agenda, to nominate candidates
for members of the board of the company, the body executive and
receive dividends). The shareholders in question were to dispose
of their shares within the time limits provided by law.
Regarding B.C. "ENERGBANK" S.A., the National Bank also found
the concerted activity of a group of shareholders with qualiﬁed
holding in the amount of 52.55% in the bank’s capital, without
the prior written permission of the NBM, the effects of the law
on the suspension of the exercise of certain shareholders’ rights
becoming incidental. These shareholders were to dispose of the
shares held in the share capital of BC "ENERGBANK" S.A. within
the time limits provided by law.
Also, as a result of the assessment carried out by the National
Bank, the Executive Board of the National Bank of Moldova decided
on 11 January 2019 to apply measures to suspend the exercise of
rights of a direct shareholder of qualiﬁed holding in the amount
of 3.98% in the share capital of BC "ENERGBANK" S.A., for his
non-compliance with the requirements regarding the adequacy
and ﬁnancial solidity, provided for in art. 48 of the Law on Banks’
Activity no. 202 of 06.10.2017. The shareholder concerned was to
alienate, within the term provided by the legislation, the shares
held in the share capital of B.C. "ENERGBANK" S.A.
Thus, taking into account the size of the package of shares
exceeding 50% of the bank’s capital, the NBM ruled on B.C.
"ENERGBANK" S.A. the application of early intervention measures
in accordance with the Law on banks recovery and resolution.
Some of the remaining members of the bank’s management body
were ﬁlled with temporary administrators appointed by the NBM in

order to ensure prudent governance of the bank’s activity during
the period of solving the deﬁciencies identiﬁed in the bank’s
ownership structure.
Subsequently, in April 2019, the Executive Board of the National
Bank of Moldova decided to extend by three months the term of
alienation by the holders of the shares of B.C. "ENERGBANK" S.A.
and, respectively, of "Banca de Finant,e s, i Comert," S.A.
In July 2019, the term of alienation of the shares held by some

s, i Comert," S.A. whose rights have been suspended following the
decisions of the NBM Executive Board, has expired.
Thus, the provisions of art. 521 of Law on Banks’ Activity no. 202
of 06.10.2017, in the part related to the procedure of cancellation
of these shares, as well as the issuance and sale of newly issued

of B.C. "ENERGBANK" S.A. and "Banca de Finant,e s, i Comert," S.A.
the suspended voting shares of "Banca de Finant,e s, i Comert," S.A.
and part of the suspended voting rights of B.C. "ENERGBANK" S.A.
were canceled, being issued new ones in the same amounts, with
the subsequent exposure to sale on the stock market for a period
of three months.
For the newly issued shares that were not alienated within three

the regulated market of newly issued shares and registered on
