Banks in liquidation
As of 31 December 2019, 5 banks were placed in forced liquidation
process. During 2019, the National Bank of Moldova approved the
Reports on completion of the liquidation process of Banca "Guinea"
S.A. and BCA "Întreprinzbanc˘a".
Regarding the liquidation process of Banca de Economii S.A., BC
"BANCA SOCIAL˘A" S.A. and BC "UNIBANK" SA, initiated in 2015,
we mention that, with the aim of recovering the funds of these
banks, during 2019, the liquidators cooperated with the criminal
investigation bodies, undertook organizational measures regarding
the sale of assets owned and in possession of banks and continued
work with debtor of banks.
As a result, at the end of 2019,
90 actions were in progress in courts in order to recover funds
previously granted by banks, totaling MDL 4,244.6 million. At the
same time, in the management of the criminal investigation bodies
there were 50 criminal cases ﬁled based on the notiﬁcations of the
liquidators.
As a result of the actions taken by the liquidators during the
liquidation process of the three banks, funds were collected in
the total amount of MDL 2,387.7 million, including in 2019 – MDL
669.3 million (Banca de Economii SA – MDL 518, 6 million, BC
"BANCA SOCIAL˘A" S.A. – MDL 132.9 million and BC "UNIBANK"
S.A. – MDL 17.8 million). The ﬁnancial resources obtained during
the liquidation process of the banks were used for the payment


Chapter 4. Banking supervision
77
of the debts related to the emergency loans granted to Banca de
Economii S.A., BC "BANCA SOCIAL˘A" S.A. and B.C. "UNIBANK" S.A.,
until the appointment of liquidators of the respective banks. Thus,
until 31 December 2019, funds amounting to MDL 2,388.9 million
were reimbursed, including in 2019 – MDL 639.0 million (Banca
de Economii S.A. – MDL 502.5 million, BC "BANCA SOCIAL˘A" S.A. –
MDL 123.5 million and BC "UNIBANK" S.A. – MDL 13.0 million).
4.3
Banking sector risks’ assessment
Macroprudential supervision
In order to prevent the accumulation of systemic risks and to
ensure the stability of the banking sector, the National Bank of
Moldova regularly performs stress tests and sensitivity studies,
which are macroprudential supervision tools for assessing the
banking sector’s resilience to potential shocks. The stress tests
used by the National Bank focus on scenarios related to credit
risk, liquidity risk, interest rate risk and exchange rate risk as well
as combinations of these. Stress testing is based on information
reported by banks and evaluates the impact of simulated shocks
in the banking sector, by groups of banks and individual banks.
As a result of the stress tests performed at the level of banking
sector, it was found that banks in the Republic of Moldova are
sufﬁciently capitalized and capable to cope with most of simulated
shocks. At the same time, banks take the necessary steps to
improve the quality of credit portfolios, which will allow them to
reduce losses in case of an adverse stress scenario.
With regard to liquidity risk, stress tests results conﬁrm the high
level of liquidity in the banking sector, the banks being able to
cope with deposits’ withdrawals at historical maximum rates, and
liquidity deﬁcits for stress test scenarios applied were not recorded.
Also, in order to assess the potential risks that could affect the
stability of the banking sector and of each bank, the National
Bank conducts the study on identifying the vulnerabilities of the
banking sector. The study’s results do not indicate an alarming
level of vulnerability of the banking sector in general and of banks
in particular.
In order to capture the opinion of risk managers within banks,
the National Bank of Moldova conducts a half-yearly survey on
systemic risk.
According to the respondents in the reference
period, the probability of materialization of key risks in the ﬁnancial
system, in the short term, was decreasing.
In order to determine the ﬁnancing conditions and the risk proﬁle
of the sectors ﬁnanced by banks in the Republic of Moldova, the
NBM conducts a quarterly survey on bank lending. The results of
the survey indicated the slight tightening trend of credit standards
and terms of loans agreements for legal entities and a slight but
persistent relaxation for individuals during 2019. According to
respondents, the main factor inﬂuencing those trends was the
pressure of competition. During the period considered, demand
for credits, both from enterprises and population, maintained an


78
Annual Report (NBM, 2019)
increasing trend. In the short term, banks anticipated a slight
increase in demand for credits from both categories of borrowers,
at the same time with a relaxation of the standards applied to
the population and a slight relaxation of the standards applied to
enterprises.
Macroprudential policy tools
In order to increase the resilience of banks to possible adverse
developments, as well as to achieve the strategic objective of
strengthening
the
function
of
ﬁnancial
stability
and
macroprudential supervision, the National Bank of Moldova as
supervisory
authority
applies
the
set
of
macroprudential
supervision tools to banks in the Republic of Moldova.
Thus, the Executive Board of the National Bank of Moldova
established the capital buffer rates applied to banks in the
Republic of Moldova at the end of 2019, as follows:
• The rate of capital conservation buffer, applied to all banks,
remained at 2.5% of the total amount of banks’ risk exposure.
• The rate of countercyclical buffer for exposures located in
the Republic of Moldova was set at 0% of banks’ exposure to
risk. The rate has been established taking into account the
absence of cyclical systemic risks related to excessive credit
growth.
• The systemic risk buffer rate for exposures located in the
Republic of Moldova was set at 1.0% or 3.0% of banks’
exposure to risk, depending on the bank’s shareholder
structure.
• In order to ensure adequate treatment of the structural risks
of the banking sector in the Republic of Moldova, the National
Bank of Moldova establishes the banks that are societies of
systemic importance. Based on this study, and in accordance
with paragraph 53 of the Regulation on Capital Buffers of
Banks, the National Bank set the rates of buffer related to the
societies of systemic importance, for banks identiﬁed as O-SII
types in the Republic of Moldova as follows:
1. 1.5% for BC "MOLDOVA-AGROINDBANK" S.A.,
2. 1.0% for BC "Moldindconbank" S.A.,
3. 1.0% for B.C. "VICTORIABANK" S.A.,
4. 0.5% for MOBIASBANCA-OTP Group S.A.
Therefore, at the end of 2019 the requirement on the own funds
rate for banks in the Republic of Moldova varies between 13.5%
and 17.0%, depending on the shareholding structure and the
systemic importance of the bank.


Chapter 4. Banking supervision
79
4.4
Fighting
money
laundering
and
terrorist ﬁnancing
The National Bank of Moldova ensures the regulation and control
of the execution of the normative framework related to the ﬁeld of
preventing
and
combating
money
laundering
and
terrorist
ﬁnancing at the level of banks, non-bank payment service
providers and foreign exchange units (other than banks).
During 2019, the National Bank of Moldova continued its activities
to strengthen the supervisory mechanism of reporting entities
in the ﬁeld of preventing and combating money laundering and
terrorist ﬁnancing in order to minimize the risks related to this ﬁeld,
both by allocating additional human resources and by continuous
improvement of the experience of NBM employees in the ﬁeld of
activity.
At the same time, the National Bank of Moldova ensured the
supervision of the reporting entities within the on-site inspections
(complex
or
thematic),
as
well
as
by
performing
remote
supervision actions (ex ofﬁcio). To this end, in order to verify the
implementation of the requirements for preventing and combating
money laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing by supervised entities,
on-site there were performed:
4 complex inspections and 3
thematic inspections at banks, 2 inspections at non-bank payment
service providers and 151 inspections at foreign exchange units.
In order to minimize risks at an early stage, supervisory actions
off-site have also been intensiﬁed, by increasing the number of
requests for information, analysis of transactions and activities of
clients of reporting entities, depending on the risk proﬁle of the
institutions and the level of risk of money laundering and terrorist
ﬁnancing associated with customers.
In the context of identifying situations of non-compliance with
the provisions of the legislation in the ﬁeld of preventing and
combating money laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing, the National
Bank of Moldova warned and made recommendations, applied
sanctions and informed the Money Laundering Prevention and
Combating Service.
In addition, during 2019, the national system for preventing and
combating money laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing was evaluated
by experts of the MONEYVAL Committee (Council of Europe), thus
drawing up the 5th Evaluation Report of the Republic of Moldova on
implementation of international standards in the ﬁeld of preventing
and combating money laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing. The
evaluation report36 is qualiﬁed as a positive aspect for the image
of the country, but also for the activity of the banking system of
the Republic of Moldova, especially related to the corresponding
relations of the licensed banks with the international institutions.
Starting from the need to identify and assess the risks of money
laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing, during September - December
2019, the 2nd external audit exercise was performed on licensed
banks
in
order
to
verify
and
assess
the
adequacy
and
36https://www.coe.int/en/web/moneyval/jurisdictions/moldova


80
Annual Report (NBM, 2019)
implementation of internal policies and procedures in the ﬁeld of
preventing
and
combating
money
laundering
and
terrorist
ﬁnancing for the period 1 September 2018 - 1 September 2019.
This exercise aims to improve the internal control system of banks
and ensures the highlighting of possible vulnerabilities at the level
of some processes or segments of activity.
Also, between 20-22 May 2019 and 4-6 June 2019, based on the
disciplinary curriculum developed within the Center of Excellence
in Economics and Finance, the FINPractice Continuing Education
Center, the representatives of the National Bank of Moldova
shared their experience and knowledge accumulated by training
commercial bank employees, non-bank payment service providers
and other participants (teachers and students) in the ﬁeld of
preventing
and
combating
money
laundering
and
terrorist
ﬁnancing.
At the same time, during the year, several actions were carried out
in order to implement the IT solution on shareholder transparency
and remote analysis in the ﬁeld of preventing and combating
money laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing, with the support of
USAID. Thus, several documents were prepared (Requests for
information, Speciﬁcations, Tender documents etc.) which aim to
prepare and initiate a public procurement, for the procurement of
the mentioned IT solution.
In accordance with good supervisory practices, the National Bank
of Moldova continued its efforts to increase cooperation activities
and exchanged information with the Money Laundering Prevention
and Combating Service and other institutions and supervisory
authorities in the Republic of Moldova and abroad, as well as
contributed to the improvement of the national framework in the
ﬁeld, by endorsing several normative acts.


81
Chapter 5
Payment system
5.1
Payment
systems
regulation
and
supervision
In accordance with the Law on the National Bank of Moldova
no. 548/1995, the regulation and supervision of ﬁnancial market
infrastructures in the Republic of Moldova is one of the core tasks
of the National Bank.
This attribution is implemented in accordance with the best
international practices, with the recommendations, principles and
standards in this domain issued by the Bank for International
Settlements,
the European Central Bank,
the International
Monetary Fund and the World Bank.
During 2019, several normative modiﬁcations took place in the
ﬁeld of payment systems. Thus, the new Regulation on automated
interbank payment system (AIPS) was approved, which includes
the general requirements for participation in AIPS, conducting
work within AIPS, risk management of the system, suspension
and termination of activity within AIPS. At the same time, the new
regulation allows the participation of non-bank payment service
providers (payment companies, electronic money issuers and
postal service providers) in AIPS.
In the context of the participation of non-bank payment service
providers in AIPS, modiﬁcations were made to the Regulation on
credit transfer, establishing their obligation to assign IBAN codes
to users’ accounts, reporting requirements for making and/or
receiving each international transfer by providing copies of
payment messages to the National Bank of Moldova (NBM).
In order to apply the provisions of art. 50 par. (6) and (7) of Law
no.114/2012 on payment services and electronic money granting
the right to the NBM to establish the amount of fees applied for
the acceptance of payment cards, as well as for operations
involving the use of AIPS, it was adopted the Decision EB no.
180/2019 establishing limits on the commission applied by
providers for accepting payments with payment cards intended
for the component budgets of the national public budget, as well
as limits on the fees applied to payers by AIPS participants for
payment orders received electronically and which involve the use
of AIPS.
According to the payment system supervision policy of the Republic
of Moldova (approved by the Executive Board Decision no. 299
of 27 October 2016), the core objective of the NBM supervision is
the promotion of efﬁciency and stability of the country’s payment


82
Annual Report (NBM, 2019)
system. In the context of the basic objective, the elements of the
payment system of the Republic of Moldova are subjected to the
National Bank supervision, whose stable and efﬁcient functioning
is essential for ﬁnancial stability, monetary policy implementation
and promoting public conﬁdence in making cashless payments.
In the domain of payments’ and settlement systems’ supervision,
the following are subjected to supervision:
1. Payment and settlement systems (high value payment
systems and high volume and low value payment systems);
2. Clearing and settlement systems of securities;
3. Money remittances;
4. Payment tools;
5. Automated remote service systems.
When exercising its oversight function, the NBM pays special
attention to systems that process, compensate and settle high
value payments and pose a substantial risk in the event of an
operational or settlement failure.
At the same time, the NBM
ensures an appropriate level of attention to all components of the
payment system subjected to supervision.
5.2
Licensing,
regulation
and
supervision
of
payment
services
providers and of electronic money
issuers
According to the Law on payment services and electronic money
no. 114/2012, the NBM licenses, regulates and supervises the
activity of payment services providers and of electronic money
issuers.
The licensing process of non-bank payment service providers
(payment companies, electronic money issuers and postal service
providers) and that of registration of payment agents takes place
in compliance with a set of requirements, which ensures a uniform
and competitive approach to access to the activity of providing
payment services and its development on the territory of the
Republic of Moldova.
As of 31 December 2019, seven non-bank payment service
providers were working in the Republic of Moldova, including one
payment company, one postal service provider and ﬁve electronic
money issuing companies.
The regulatory framework elaborated by the NBM in the
context of the activity of providing payment services comes to
align the internal regulations of the proﬁle with the European
rules,
materializing in the adoption of the normative acts
necessary to carry out the activity on the payment services
market. This framework is an essential support for increasing the


Chapter 5. Payment system
83
number of modern and secure payment services, increasing
competition in the payment services market due to the market
access of non-banking providers, thus promoting better efﬁciency
and quality, reducing service costs and achieving a high level of
non-cash payments and reduction of cash in circulation.
In order to connect to the provisions of Law no. 208/2018 for the
amendment of some legislative acts and in the context of the
implementation of the IT solution for streamlining the licensing,
authorization and notiﬁcation process, in 2019 a new Regulation on
the activity of non-bank payment service providers was approved.
At the same time, according to the provisions of Law no. 208/2018,
in 2019 was approved Regulation on the credit activity of non-
banking payment service providers, according to which the area of
activity of non-banking payment service providers was extended,
by allowing to grant credits to users of their services, only if those
credits are exclusively related to payment services and only in the
case of facilitation of payment services.
In order to improve the regulations for non-banking payment
service providers related to the requirements, conditions and
restrictions on acquiring/increasing qualiﬁed participations in their
share capital,
detailing the criteria for evaluating potential
acquirers of qualiﬁed participations,
as well as monitoring
shareholder quality, in 2019 was approved the Regulation on
shareholdings in non-bank payment service providers.
It was also approved the Regulation regarding on-site inspections
for non-bank payment service providers and the criteria for
applying remedies and sanctions, which contains provisions on
informing providers on how to carry out on-site inspections, the
rights and the obligations of the parties, the issues related to the
reporting of the results of the inspections, the criteria for the
application of remedial measures and sanctions.
In 2019, 2 on-site inspections for licensed banks were carried
out, during which the banks’ activity was veriﬁed in accordance
with the legislation related to payment systems.
The ex ofﬁcio supervision of the banks’ activity was also carried out,
by examining the action plans for eliminating the infringements
and remedying the deﬁciencies found in the previous inspections.
During 2019, four inspections were carried out, of which three
on-site inspections and an ex ofﬁcio inspection, in which the
activity
of
licensed
non-banking
payment
service
providers was veriﬁed.
As a result of the inspections carried out,
proposals and
recommendations were made on the actions needed to be taken
in order to eliminate the breaches and shortcomings detected and,
where
appropriate,
remedial
measures
were
imposed
and
sanctions applied.
The NBM also carried out ex ofﬁcio monitoring of the activity of
payment
service
providers,
including
through
continuous
monitoring
of
their
activity,
analysis
of
quarterly
reports
submitted to the NBM, examination of notiﬁcations (complaints)
related to the activity of payment service providers, examination
of information published on web pages etc.

5.3
Automated
interbank
payment
system
The automated interbank payment system (AIPS) is the system
through which payments are made automatically in Moldovan lei
of/through participants on the territory of the Republic of Moldova.
The AIPS consists of the real-time gross settlement system (the
RTGS system) and the designated-time net settlement system (the
DNS system). The RTGS system is designed to process urgent and
high value payments, and the DNS system is designed to process
small value payments.
At the end of 2019, 17 participants were registered in AIPS, namely:
• the National Bank of Moldova;
• 11 banks licensed by the National Bank of Moldova;
• Cash and settlement center in Tiraspol city;
• The State Treasury within the Ministry of Finance, including
as a mandated participant;
• The Single Central Depository of Securities S.A.;
• Deposit Guarantee Fund in Banking System.
In 2019, through AIPS, a total volume of 13.6 million transactions
worth of MDL 1.0 billion were processed, increasing in number and
decreasing in value by 4.7% and 14.5%, respectively, compared
to 2018. The average daily volume37 of operations amounted to
53.4 thousand transactions worth MDL 4.0 billion. The maximum
volume of payments settled daily in AIPS was reached on 24
December 2019, being settled 98.9 thousand payments.

it is found that payment transactions made by participants on

(only 33.5% of the value of settled transactions).

Chapter 5. Payment system
85
• ordinary customer transfers – 52.7% of total payments;
• transfers related to budget payments – 45.2%;

In terms of value, transactions in AIPS, made on behalf of clients
have the following structure:
• the amount of ordinary customer transfers – 62.8% of total
payments;
• budget transfers – 35.7%;
• other types of payments – 1.5%.
During 2019, the average availability of the system for participants
was 99.9%, which illustrates the extent to which AIPS participants
had the ability to use AIPS without incident during the operating
program.
5.4
The Single Central Depository of
Securities
The
Single
Central
Securities
Depository
(CSD)
system
is
operational since 31 July 2018 and is administered (managed and
operated) by the single Central Securities Depository.
The CSD system is a securities settlement system, which ensures
the provision by the Central Depository of securities registration,
centralized administration, settlement and, where appropriate,
ancillary services. The CSD system is based on a modern direct
processing technology, connected to AIPS and offers an efﬁcient
infrastructure and services for the settlement of securities
transactions and the registration of property rights over securities.
At the end of 2019, there were registered 20 participants in CSD,
of which:
• The National Bank of Moldova;
• 11 banks licensed by the National Bank of Moldova;
• Deposit Guarantee Fund in the Banking System;
• 7 investment companies.
In accordance with Law no.
234/2016 on the single Central
Depository of Securities, during 2019 the registry companies and
issuers
that
kept
the
register
of
holders
of
securities
independently submitted to CSD, until 1 May 2019, the registers
of holders of securities of banks and insurance companies. The
registers of the holders of securities of the entities admitted for
trading on the regulated market and within a multilateral trading
system,
other than banks and insurance companies,
were
submitted to CSD until 15 December 2019.


86
Annual Report (NBM, 2019)
Securities registration services provided by CSD
At the end of 2019, 240.4 million securities of SS and NBC type
were registered in the CSD system at a nominal value of MDL 28.9
billion.
At the same time, according to the data presented by CSD at
the end of 2019, 290.6 million corporate securities (CS) were
registered in the CSD system, out of which 288.7 million ordinary
shares and 1.9 million preferential shares. The total value of CS
constituted for the reference period MDL 5.6 billion, of which 99.9%
of the total value of CS represented ordinary shares.
Settlement services
During 2019, 6.4 thousand transfer orders worth MDL 315.6 billion
were settled in the CSD system.
From May 2019, within the
CSD system, transactions on the corporate securities market
began to be settled, such as: stock exchange transactions, OTC
transactions.
5.5
Cashless payment tools
Cashless payment tools are essential components of the payment
system and the National Bank conducts their supervision in order
to ensure efﬁciency and security of their utilization.
Out of payment tools available, the most used by population
(individuals) are payment cards.
It is worth mentioning that
the NBM is undertaking relevant measures together with other
national authorities and institutions in order to promote utilization
of cashless payment tools and ﬁght tax evasion.
Payment cards
Number of cards38 in circulation at the end of 2019 constituted
2.0 million units and increased by 3.9% compared to end of 2018,
reaching the highest level so far (Chart 5.3).
On the background of the increase in the number of cards in
circulation, the number of active cards39 had an increase by 3.2%
compared to the end of 2018 and the share of active cards in the
total number of cards in circulation did not change signiﬁcantly,

replacement of hybrid cards, which in turn substituted the

The value of all transactions made through ARSS by individuals
and legal entities totalled MDL 565.6 billion in 2019, increasing
by 17.3% from the previous year, which shows the increase in
customer conﬁdence in transactions through ARSS (Chart 5.9).
Approximately 98.1% of the value of the transactions performed

Credit transfer
Of the total number of payment transactions carried out through
credit
transfer
initiated
by
bank
customers
(19.3
million
transactions), 74.4% were initiated in electronic format through
ARSS. Over the last 5 years, this indicator has improved from
48.0% for 2015 to 74.4% for 2019, which means an increase in
the digitization level of payment services in the Republic of
Moldova.
Also, in relative terms, the number of electronically
initiated credit transfers grew by 24.0%, compared to the previous
year while the number of paper-based decreased by 2.8%
compared to 2018. At the same time, the value of operations
carried out by credit transfer initiated by the clients of the banks
reached the amount of MDL 502.8 billion, of which 69.8% were
made in electronic format, and 30.2% on paper.

executed in a total amount of MDL 67.1 million, increasing in
number by 28.7% and in value by 57.9%. The total number of
users of this payment instrument constituted at the end of 2019
about 8.4 thousand, twice more compared to the previous year.

Evolution of currency (banknotes
and coins in circulation)
The value of banknotes in circulation at the end of 2019, amounted
to MDL 25,677.9 million, an increase of 8.8% compared to the end
of the previous year. The evolution of the value of banknotes in
circulation is shown in the chart 6.1.
At the end of 2019, about 284.5 million banknotes were in
circulation, by 0.2% more than at the end of 2018. The evolution
of banknotes in monetary circulation, in terms of quantity, by the
amount of each denomination is presented in the chart 6.2.
Quantitative increase of banknotes in circulation was determined
by a higher increase in the quantity of banknotes with the
following denomination: 100 lei, 500 lei and 1,000 lei.
Banknotes with the denomination of 1 leu continued to hold the
largest share of total number of banknotes in circulation, 30.8%,
followed by banknotes with the denomination of 200 lei, with
a share of 23.6% and that of 100 lei, with a share of 14.0%.
Similar to the previous years, the smallest share in the total
number of banknotes in circulation belonged to banknotes with
the denomination of MDL 1000 (0.8%).
According to the nominal value structure, at the end of the
reporting
period,
the
quantity
of
banknotes
in
circulation
constituted: of 1 leu – 87.6 million banknotes, 5 lei – 17.8 million
banknotes, 10 lei – 19.4 million banknotes, 20 lei – 12.5 million
banknotes, 50 lei – 30.6 million banknotes, 100 lei – 39.7 million
banknotes, 200 lei – 67.0 million banknotes, 500 lei – 7.6 million
banknotes and 1000 lei – 2.3 million banknotes.
The chart 6.3 reﬂects details on the structure of banknotes in
circulation at the end of 2019, by nominal values, represented in

and value, being 3.8% higher compared to these values at the
end of 2018. The evolution of coins in the monetary circulation
according to the nominal value structure is presented, in terms of
value and quantity, in the chart 6.4 and 6.5, respectively.

The National Bank of Moldova is continuously monitoring the
maintenance of the quality of the banknotes in circulation,
according to the criteria set by the NBM. Thus, 68.6 million
banknotes
were
processed
in

National currency counterfeiting
During 2019, the number of counterfeits of national currency in the
banking system totaled 2.5 thousand banknotes, about 24.7% less
than in 2018. As a fraction of the number of genuine banknotes in
circulation at the end of the reference period, which constituted
285 million banknotes, the number of forged banknotes was at a
very low level and represented 0.0009% or about 9 fake banknotes
per 1 million genuine banknotes in circulation.
The most frequently forged banknote was that of 100 lei – 2.1
thousand counterfeits. On the second place was the banknote
with the denomination of 20 lei, with 249 counterfeits, followed by
the 200 lei banknote with the number of 48 counterfeits, the 50
lei banknote, with 41 counterfeits and the 1000 lei banknote, with
a number of 21 counterfeits.
As a basis for comparison, in 2018, the number of counterfeits
of national currency amounted to 3.3 thousand banknotes, and
the highest number of counterfeits were recorded for the 100
lei banknotes, totaling 2.7 thousand banknotes, 20 lei – 296
banknotes, 200 lei – 217 banknotes, 50 lei – 53 banknotes, 1000
lei – 22 banknotes. The share of fake banknotes on face value in
2019 and 2018 is shown in the chart 6.15 and 6.16, respectively.
In order to ﬁght counterfeiting of national currency, the National
Bank of Moldova further recommends the public become familiar
with the safety features of banknotes on the NBM website43. The
public should also be vigilant about the counterfeiting phenomenon
while making daily payments in cash by checking the authenticity
of banknotes and coins, and in case of suspicion, to communicate
immediately such cases to the Ministry of Internal Affairs.
At the same time, the NBM regularly co-operates with the Ministry
of Internal Affairs in order to withdraw the counterfeit banknotes,
discourage and maintain the phenomenon of counterfeiting at the
lowest level and ensures the operative brieﬁng of the banking
environment on the methods for detecting and extracting
counterfeits from circulation.

Chapter 7
Legal regulatory framework
7.1
Prudential regulation, regulation of
accounting evidence in banks
During 2019, in order to apply the provisions of Law no. 202/2017
on Banks’ Activity, the National Bank of Moldova:
• Approved
the
Regulation
on
large
exposures
which
transposes the provisions of the European framework. The
Regulation
sets
out
requirements
for
calculating
the
exposure value, maximum permissible limits on the risk of
concentrating the bank’s exposures to a client or group of
connected clients, concentrations in the exposure portfolio,
and requirements for monitoring, managing and reporting
large exposures. The new elements of the regulation are: the
deﬁnition of the capital on which the limits are based; the
methods
for
calculating
the
exposure
value;
the
determination
of
the
exposures
exempted
from
the
application of the limits established by the regulation; the
treatment
of
credit
risk
mitigation
techniques
when
calculating the exposure value, as well as the methodology
for identifying the group of connected clients.
For the purpose of ensuring the bank’s ability to identify
and manage the large exposures, it is required to dispose of
internal regulations, rigorous administrative and accounting
procedures and adequate internal control mechanisms to
enable it to identify, manage, monitor, report and record
large exposures to a client or group of connected clients.
The provisions of the regulation contribute to limit the
concentration of credit risk and therefore to minimize the
maximum losses that a bank could incur if the counterparty
or the group of connected clients could encounter difﬁculties
in meeting the obligations as a debtor.
The new requirements for large exposures have led to the
need of approval of amendments to the Instruction on
presentation by Banks of COREP reports for supervisory
purposes, which contributes to the uniform and timely
reporting by banks of information regarding the exposures to
a client or group of connected clients and, respectively, to
improving the supervision of the risks of concentrating the
bank’s exposures.
• Amended the Regulation on Banks’ Transactions with Their
Related Parties. The proposed amendments and adjustments
focused in particular on the following issues:
– determining the exposures value to related parties similar
to those provided for in the Regulation on large exposures;


Chapter 7. Legal regulatory framework
97
– bringing in line with the terms and provisions in the context
of Law no.
202/2017 and of the Regulation on large
exposures;
– improving the process of presuming certain parties as
related to the bank;
– re-examining and improving the characteristics used by the
National Bank to identify the parties that have relations or
perform transactions with the bank and/or its related parties,
following the supervision practice;
– setting minimum requirements for internal regulations and
risk management practices associated to transactions with
bank related parties.
• Approved amendments to the Regulation on the open foreign
exchange position, generated by the substitution of terms
used in accordance with IAS 39 "Financial Instruments:
Recognition
and
Measurement"
with
those
used
in
accordance
with
IFRS
9
"Financial
Instruments”,
i.e.
classiﬁcation of ﬁnancial instruments and modiﬁcation of
impairment provisions related to the accounting for expected
losses.
Respectively, the notions used according to the
provisions of the Regulation on Banks’ Own Funds and
Capital Requirements and other regulatory acts in force have
been updated.
• Approved amendments to the Regulation on Bank’s liquidity,
which aimed to change the method of calculating the liquidity
indicator on maturity bands (Principle III) by determining
the surplus of liquidity.
As a result, the approval of the
Regulation on Bank’s liquidity allowed for a reporting adjusted
in accordance with the requirements of the legal framework
for maintaining an adequate level of liquidity.
• Approved the Regulation on Branches and Secondary Ofﬁces
of Banks, which establishes the manner of opening and
closing branches and secondary ofﬁces of banks in the
Republic of Moldova.
The approval of the Regulation on
Branches and Secondary Ofﬁces of Banks ensures the
creation of the secondary regulatory framework for opening
branches in other states and simpliﬁes the process of
opening and closing branches and secondary ofﬁces in the
country, which positively inﬂuences the expansion of banks
in the territory.
• Approved Regulation on Licensing of Bank and of a Branch of
a Bank from Another State. The regulation is based on the
new
provisions
of
Law
no.
202/2017
which
impose
requirements on access to the activity of banks, including
the licensing of banks and the regime of branches of banks
from another states.
• Approved amendments to the the list of jurisdictions that do
not implement the international standards of transparency.
The list was supplemented with 16 jurisdictions. The purpose
of the amendments is the non-admission of direct or indirect
acquisition/acquirement of holdings in a bank in the Republic
of Moldova by resident persons in jurisdictions that do not
implement the international standards of transparency, as
well as the non-admission to establish branches of banks in
those jurisdictions on the territory of the Republic of Moldova.


98
Annual Report (NBM, 2019)
The National Bank of Moldova continues the development of
regulatory acts for the execution of Law no. 202/2017 on Banks’
Activity.
Aligning the banking legislation of the Republic of Moldova with
international
standards
by
improving
the
quantitative
and
qualitative mechanisms of bank management will contribute to
promoting a secure and stable banking sector,
increasing
transparency, trust and attractiveness of the domestic banking
sector for potential investors and creditors of banks as well as for
depositors and clients, in the development of new ﬁnancial
products and services.
7.2
Currency regulation
In 2019, the National Bank of Moldova promoted a series of
regulatory acts (including amendments) targeting the foreign
exchange ﬁeld, aimed at improving the legal framework related to
foreign currency, in particular on some issues related to the export
and import of cash and traveller’s checks from / into the Republic
of Moldova by banks, as well as on issues related to the activity of
foreign exchange units.
• It was approved Regulation on some issues related to the
export and import of cash and traveller’s checks by banks,
which replaces the old regulatory act, namely the Instruction
on the introduction / withdrawal of cash in / from the
Republic of Moldova by banks.
The regulation aims to
improve the regulatory framework of the National Bank of
Moldova regarding the export and import of cash from / into
the Republic of Moldova by banks, following the amendments
to Law no. 62/2008 on Foreign Exchange Regulation aimed at
liberalizing foreign exchange operations, as well as the
decision of the National Bank and of the Customs Service for
the separate regulation of the aspects which refer to the ﬁeld
of competence of each authority related to the export /
import of cash by banks.
Among the most important provisions of the Regulation, the
following might be highlighted:
– establishing the conditions related to the export and import
from / into the Republic of Moldova of cash in national
currency and in foreign currency and traveller’s checks in
foreign currency by resident and non-resident banks;
– regulation of cash export / import operations of licensed
banks carried out for the purpose of testing automatic coin
and banknote processing devices in the national currency;
–
establishing
the
criteria
to
be
met
by
specialized
non-resident legal entities with which the licensed bank
could establish contractual relations aimed at importing /
exporting cash into / from the Republic of Moldova (for
example,
specialized non-resident legal entity with an
uncompromising reputation);
– regulation of the manner by which the National Bank issues
authorizations for the export / import of cash in the national
currency;


Chapter 7. Legal regulatory framework
99
– establishing the obligation of the bank to take measures
to ensure the security of the process related to the export /
import of cash and traveller’s checks.
• With a view to adjust certain regulatory acts of the National
Bank of Moldova in the context of amendments to the
legislation of the Republic of Moldova such as Law no.
62/2008 on Foreign Exchange Regulation (elimination of the
obligation of licensed banks to apply cash and control
machines when carrying out foreign exchange operations
through its foreign exchange ofﬁces), to other regulatory
acts concerning the activity of foreign exchange units (such
as Government Decision no. 141/2019 on the application of
cash
and
control
equipment
to
settlements,
etc.),
amendments were promoted to the Regulation on the
Activity of Foreign Exchange Entities and to Regulation on
Licensing, Control and Sanctioning of Foreign Exchange
Entities.
In particular, the mentioned regulatory acts excluded the
provisions regarding the application by the licensed banks of
cash and control machines when carrying out cash exchange
operations with individuals through foreign exchange ofﬁces.
At the same time, the amendments to the Regulation on the
Activity of Foreign Exchange Entities aim at establishing the
obligation of the licensed bank to register in its information
system the cash exchange operations carried out through
foreign exchange ofﬁces, as well as to issue a document
conﬁrming the transaction in question, namely the foreign
exchange receipt to clients who carry out foreign exchange
transactions in cash.
Among the most important amendments to the Regulation
on Licensing, Control and Sanctioning of Foreign Exchange
Entities might be mentioned:
– the speciﬁcations for the annual presentation of a single
original criminal record, if one and the same person exercises
the function of administrator or deputy administrator and at
the same time the function of chief accountant and / or the
person in question has the quality of associate / shareholder
of one exchange ofﬁce or several exchange ofﬁces;
– the exclusion of some provisions regarding the minimum
requirements for the rooms of the exchange ofﬁces, these
being the object of regulation of regulatory acts of the
Government of the Republic of Moldova.


100
Annual Report (NBM, 2019)
Chapter 8
International relations
8.1
International Collaboration
International Monetary Fund (IMF)44
During 2019, the cooperation between the Republic of Moldova and
the IMF have mainly developed in the context of implementation of
the IMF-supported Program through the EFF ﬁnancing mechanisms
(Extended Fund Facility45) and the ECF (Extended Credit Facility46),
which was approved by the Board of Executive Directors of the
IMF on 7 November 2016 initially for a period of 3 years, with a
subsequent extension until March 2020, in order to support the
state’s economic and ﬁnancial reform agenda.
The agenda of the IMF-supported Program in 2019 continued to
focus on reforms in the ﬁnancial and banking sector, prudent
management of public ﬁnances and improving the operating
framework in the energy sector. Thus, in the banking sector, the
Program aimed at strengthening corporate governance and
increasing shareholder transparency, improving risk management
and addressing issues related to crediting the related parties and
streamlining
the
activity
of
the
single
Central
Securities
Depository.
The dialogue with the International Monetary Fund, in 2019, took
place, including, during the working visits of IMF experts in the
Republic of Moldova.
The ﬁrst working visit of IMF representatives took place on 25-29
March 2019 and took place outside the standard assessment cycle,
in order to assess the progress made in the context of maintaining
macroeconomic and budgetary and ﬁscal stability. Such working
visits outside the standard program assessment cycle are part
of the IMF’s usual practice when it comes to countries with IMF-
supported programs.
The second visit of IMF experts took place between 26 June –
10 July, 2019 and took place in the context of the 4th and 5th
assessments of the IMF-supported ﬁnancing program related to the
level of implementation of the assumed conditionalities through
this Program. During this visit, IMF representatives noted that
44http://bnm.md/en/content/relatia-republicii-moldova-cu-fondul-monetar-
international-fmi
45Extended Fund Facility (EFF) which stipulates an annual interest rate equal to
the SDRs’ core interest rate, a 10-years reimbursement term and a grace period of
four and a half years.
46Extended Credit Facility (ECF) which stipulates an interest rate equal to zero
until the end of 2018, a grace period of ﬁve and a half years and a repayment
period of 10 years. Two thirds of the loan amount is granted on ECF basis.


Chapter 8. International relations
101
the Program’s commitments are broadly in line with the agreed
parameters, with the full involvement of the authorities, and
that the authorities have taken the necessary steps to eliminate
deviations from the IMF program IMF and achieve the objectives
of ensuring macroeconomic stability and advancing reforms. At
the same time, signiﬁcant progress was made in implementing
the reform agenda in the ﬁnancial sector, completing important
measures to ensure shareholder transparency and compliance
with the requirements in the banking sector, improving supervisory
and regulatory frameworks, eliminating wide bank exposures to
their related parties and strengthening mechanisms to ensure
ﬁnancial stability. Following the completion of the fourth and ﬁfth
assessments of the Program, in 2019, the Republic of Moldova
obtained access to the amount of SDR 33.6 million (about USD
46.0 million), which represents the the ﬁfth disbursement under
the current ECF/ EFF Program totaling SDR 129.4 million (about
USD 178.7 million or 75% of Moldova’s share in the IMF).
The third working visit of IMF experts took place on 2 – 8 October
2019, and the purpose of the mission was to examine measures
and reforms aimed at contributing to the implementation of the
2020 budgetary-tax policy package.
In the context of the ﬁnancial arrangements, during 2019, the
NBM received from the IMF an amount of SDR 9 million (USD 12.3
million), provided through the EFF ﬁnancing mechanism, and an
amount of SDR 4.5 million (USD 6.14 million), provided through the
ECF ﬁnancing mechanism, for the consolidation of the international
reserves of the Republic of Moldova.
According to the decision approved by the IMF Executive Board
on 7 November 2016 on ﬁnancial arrangements, by the end of
2019 the NBM beneﬁted from SDR 44.4 million in the context of
the extended ﬁnancing facility and SDR 4.5 million, in the context
of the extended lending facility, which is the equivalent of the
approved amounts.
In 2019, the total payments related to the servicing of loans
granted to the NBM by the IMF amounted to SDR 42.1 million
(equivalent to USD 58.3 million), of which payments for the
principal amount of the loan amounted to SDR 40 million
(equivalent to USD 55.3 million), and interest payments on loans
amounted to SDR 2.1 million (equivalent to USD 2.9 million).
As of 31 December 2019, the balance of ﬁnancial commitments to
the IMF, which beneﬁted the NBM, amounted to: EFF – SDR 93.7
million (USD 129.87 million) and ECF – 36.83 (USD 51.02 million).
At the same time, it is noteworthy that in 2019, the NBM received
assistance from the IMF in order to improve the framework for
implementing monetary policy and ﬁnancial stability, as well as, in
other areas speciﬁc to the central bank.

European Union (EU)47
In order to fulﬁll the commitments provided in the Association

under its responsibility, reﬂected in the National Action Plan of
the Republic of Moldova – EU Association Agreement (NAPAA)
for 2017-2019, approved by the Government Decision of the
Republic of Moldova no. 1472 of 30 December 2016. Thus, in
the context of PNAAA, the NBM undertook to contribute to its
implementation through a series of actions, such as: aligning
central bank legislation with EU best practices, strengthening the
NBM’s regulatory and supervisory capacity banking, developing
and consolidating the requirements related to the governance and
management of banking risk, transposition and implementation of
EU legislation in the ﬁeld of ﬁnancial-banking services, etc.
It should be noted that in order to fulﬁll the commitments in the
Association Agreement, the NBM beneﬁts from technical support
from the EU through its assistance instruments.

the

Commission
for
European

in the implementation of the NAPAA for 2017-2019, as well as, the

One of the key priorities of the NBM in implementing the

country in the Eastern Partnership to accede to this memorandum.
This achievement was possible after in 2018 EBA positively

World Bank
In 2019, the NBM maintained a dialogue with the World Bank,
during which all the necessary information for the fair and objective
assessment of the situation in the domestic ﬁnancial system was
presented to this institution, in order to make decisions to continue
granting ﬁnancing in various priority areas for the Republic of
Moldova.
At the same time, it should be noted that the World Bank regularly

European

Association
(EACHA)49
During 2019, the NBM continued to work with EACHA, through the
exchange of information and experience in the ﬁeld of payment
processing houses. From 21 to 22 May 2019, the representatives
of NBM participated in the annual "EACHA Payments Forum 2019"
and in the EACHA plenary session.
During the events, there
were discussed aspects related to the role of the clearing and
settlement mechanism in a changing ﬁnancial environment, details
related to the development of instant payments in the European
area, convergence between instant payments and payment cards,
aspects of European legislation, including features of Directive
2015/2366 on payment services in the European internal market
(PSD2), the experience of the North countries in the P27 project,
as well as other related topics. Participation in these events took
place in the context of achieving the strategic objectives of the
NBM, namely, the accession of the banking sector to the SEPA area
and the implementation of instant payments.

In 2019, the NBM continued to participate in the activities carried
out by IORWG, by providing answers to requests and studies
initiated by the group. At the same time, the NBM was a member
of the Expert Group for the 14th Annual Conference in May 2019,
organized by the Central Bank of the Republic of Chile, on the
following topics:
• Risk management function in central banks;
• Monetary and supervisory authorities;
• Risk repository.
48http://bnm.md/en/content/relatia-republicii-moldova-cu-banca-europeana-
pentru-reconstructie-si-dezvoltare-berd
49European Association of Automated Clearing Houses (EACHA) is a non-proﬁt
organization of European Union (EU) clearing house and payment processing
system administrators and European Economic Area (EEA). EACHA organizes
several working groups in the payments industry to address issues such as
interoperability within the Single European Payments Area (SEPA). EACHA currently
consists of 27 members from 23 different countries, of which 20 are from the
EU (Germany, France, Austria, Italy, Sweden, etc.) and 2 from the EEA (Norway,
Switzerland). The National Bank of Moldova, as an administrator of the Automated
Interbank Payment System (SAPI), became a member of EACHA in 2014.)
50International Operational Risk Working Group – IORWG) is a working group for
central banks and monetary and supervisory authorities, which aims to exchange
experience, innovate the methodological framework and share best practices
between central banks in the ﬁeld of operational risk management. The National
Bank of Moldova became a member of the IORWG in 2015.

consolidation of the status of the Republic of Moldova as a

which discussed the topics "Challenges of banking supervision in

51CEF is an international organization with the mission of supporting the
implementation of reforms in the ﬁelds of public ﬁnance and ﬁnancial-banking in
Southeastern Europe through training activities. CEF was set up in 2001 under the
Stability Pact for Southeastern Europe by the Slovak government, at the initiative
of the Slovenian Ministry of Finance and in close cooperation with other ﬁnance
ministries of the former Yugoslav countries and Albania. In 2015, CEF became an
international organization after 14 years of success as a regional institution.
52The
International
Organization
of
La
Francophonie
is
an
international
organization that, based on the afﬁnity for the French language, brings together
states and governments on ﬁve continents: 53 states and governments with
membership full, 2 with associate membership status and 13 with observer status.
The intergovernmental nucleus of the Francophonie was created on 20 March
1970, with the establishment of the Agency for Cultural and Technical Cooperation
(ACCT). The ACCT, whose title and portfolio have evolved over time, became
the "Intergovernmental Agency of the Francophonie" (IDA) in 1999. For better
visibility and coherence on the international stage, in January 2006, the IDA
became the "International Organization of the Francophonie". (OIF), as the only
intergovernmental body of the Francophonie. The Republic of Moldova became a
member of the OIF in 1996.
53The Group of Banking Supervisors from Central and Eastern Europe (BSCEE) is a
regional organization without legal personality that promotes cooperation between
banking supervisors of European countries. Central and Eastern. The National Bank
of Moldova became a member of the BSCEE in 1996, following the signing of the
Agreement on the rules of organization and management of the BSCEE Group.

Therefore, in order to achieve the strategic objective, improve the
monetary policy framework and optimize the implementation of
monetary policy, the NBM continued to beneﬁt from the assistance
of the International Monetary Fund. In this regard, during 2019, IMF
experts carried out a series of missions at the NBM headquarters,
with the following themes:
• Improving the inﬂation targeting framework, conducted
between 16 January and 7 February 2019;
• Strengthening of monetary and foreign exchange operations
under the inﬂation targeting regime, conducted between 20

and

independence of the NBM in order to strengthen monetary

operations
under

missions
contribute
to
streamlining
the
implementation
of

targeting regime, as well as, to strengthening the long-term
ﬁnancial independence of the NBM.


Chapter 8. International relations
107
In order to improve the capacity and efﬁciency of the NBM’s
function to produce and provide publications and analytical
materials containing national statistics, the NBM also received
technical assistance from the IMF during 2018-2019.
Thus, between 2018 and 2019, IMF experts conducted a series of
working visits to the Republic of Moldova to provide support to the
NBM, in order to develop an aggregate indicator of the dynamics
of residential real estate prices in the Republic of Moldova. As a
result of these missions, the Residential Real Estate Price Indicator
was developed experimentally, as well as, optional technical
recommendations were provided in order to improve the quality of
data on the real estate market in the Republic of Moldova.
At the same time, with the support of the IMF, the NBM revised
the ﬁnancial soundness indicators. The recommendations made
in the assistance allowed the NBM to improve the quality of the
ﬁnancial soundness indicators reported to the IMF, as well as, to
reconsider the available data source to ensure the coherence of
the indicators with the IMF Compilation Guide.
In 2019, the NBM continued to beneﬁt from the assistance of a
senior ﬁnancial sector adviser in the context of the new European
Union (EU) Public Policy Advisory Program for the Republic of
Moldova, for 2019-2021, advising the NBM in the ﬁeld of banking
supervision and the implementation of risk-based supervision,
effective communication, as well as, to facilitate cooperation with
various local and international authorities.
At the same time, within the same Program, the NBM, starting with
1 October 2019, beneﬁts from the assistance of a medium-term EU
expert for banking supervision from the National Bank of Romania
(BNR), in order to strengthen the knowledge of banking supervisors
effective risk-based supervision and professional judgment, using
the Supervisory Review and Evaluation Process (SREP). Due to the
support provided by the expert, the structure and content of the
SREP reports of all banks in the Republic of Moldova is improved.
Through the European Commission’s Technical Assistance and
Information Exchange Instrument (TAIEX)54, in 2019, the NBM
received the following assistance in the areas of banking
supervision, ﬁnancial stability, combating money laundering and
terrorist ﬁnancing and cash management, as follows:
• Mission of the experts of the National Bank of Romania
regarding supervision and assessment of operational risk,
organized between 8 – 12 April 2019;
• Mission of the experts of the Central Bank of Lithuania on
assessment of market risk and interest rate risk outside the
trading book, organized from 22 to 26 April 2019;
• Mission of the experts of the Central Bank of Lithuania on
supervision and assessment of credit and counterparty risks,
organized from 20 to 24 May 2019;
54TAIEX is a European Commission Technical Assistance and Information Exchange
Instrument which aims to support beneﬁciary authorities in the process of
harmonizing national legislation with EU. TAIEX assistance is offered to short-term
(up to 5 days) beneﬁciary public authorities in the form of workshops, missions of EU
experts to beneﬁciary institutions or study visits by representatives of beneﬁciary
authorities to EU authorities.

of the NBM were also invited. The forum aimed to bring together
representatives of central banks from Eastern Europe, the Balkans
and the Black Sea region to share their experience in the ﬁeld
of internal audit, with a special focus on information technology
auditing.
In order to get acquainted with the practice of the Central Bank
of Lithuania in the ﬁnancial-accounting ﬁeld, between 12 and
14 November 2019, the NBM representatives paid a fact-ﬁnding
visit to the headquarters of the Central Bank of Lithuania. The
materials provided during the mission, as well as the experience
gained, will serve to improve the activity of the NBM subdivision
with competencies in this ﬁeld, a process carried out during the
years 2020-2021.
The National Bank of Moldova constantly participates in the
process of promoting ﬁnancial education at the national level,
together with other relevant institutions. In this regard, starting
with 2018, the authorities of the Republic of Moldova, together
with those of other countries (Bulgaria, Croatia, Georgia, Northern
Macedonia, Montenegro and Romania) beneﬁt from the technical
expertise of the Money Wise platform in the Kingdom of the
Netherlands, in the context of the Five-Year Project (2018-2022)
for technical assistance in the ﬁeld of ﬁnancial education under
the District Program of the Ministry of Finance of the Kingdom of
the
Netherlands,
led
by
the
Organization
for
Economic
Co-operation and Development (OECD) and its International
Financial Education Network (INFE).
In 2019, OECD experts, based on the questionnaires for evaluating
ﬁnancial education in the Republic of Moldova, completed by
representatives of public authorities in the Republic of Moldova,
licensed banks, non-banking sector and civil society prepared the
Preliminary Report on Mapping Financial Education in the Republic
of Moldova.
At the same time, the NBM and the National Commission for
Financial Markets (NCFM) with the support of experts contracted
through the draft Good Governance Fund Program, implemented
during the period 1 October 2019 – 15 February 2020, developed
a series of information materials, which reﬂect the more important
ﬁnancial concepts and products such as: inﬂation, effective annual
interest, consumer credit, car leasing, insurance, etc.
In 2019, at the request of external partners, the National Bank of
Moldova provided external technical assistance to other central
banks, by sharing its experience and practices in various areas of
activity speciﬁc to a central bank.
Thus, the NBM shared its experience in the ﬁeld of international
account statistics with colleagues from the Central Bank of


112
Annual Report (NBM, 2019)
Botswana during a visit of the representatives of these institutions
to the NBM. During the visit, discussions were held on various
modern aspects of compiling and using statistics, such as: the
transition to the international standard MBP6, methods of data
collection,
processing and dissemination,
identiﬁcation and
extension of their sources and information technologies applied in
this ﬁeld.
At the same time, between 15 and 16 October 2019, the National
Bank of Moldova held a workshop entitled "The experience of the
National Bank of Moldova in implementing International Financial
Reporting Standards (IFRS) in the banking sector", an event jointly
organized with the World Bank. The purpose of the workshop
was to share the NBM experience with the representatives of
the National Bank of the Republic of Belarus on the transition
of the banking sector in Moldova to IFRS, focusing on issues
related to accounting, banking supervision, statistics, accounting
methodology and IT.


113
Chapter 9
Institutional
and
organizational
framework
9.1
Corporate
Governance
of
the
National Bank
The Law on the National Bank of Moldova provides the legal
authority and the governance framework of the National Bank.
The structure of corporate governance includes governing bodies,
committees that support governing bodies, and external and
internal control levels. This structure is complemented by the
compliance and ethics framework, which includes the Code of
Conduct for NBM Employees, the Anti-Fraud Policy of the National
Bank of Moldova, the Information Security Policy, as well as the
public access to normative acts, statistics and publications of the
NBM.
Governing bodies of the National Bank
Governor
The Governor of the National Bank is appointed by the Parliament
at the proposal of the President of the Parliament.
In accordance with the Law on the National Bank of Moldova,
the governor is responsible for formulating monetary and foreign
exchange policy initiatives for submission to the Executive Board,
as well as their execution.
Also, the governor organizes and
manages the activity of the bank and represents it in relations with
any legal or natural person both in the Republic of Moldova and
abroad.
Supervisory Board
The Supervisory Board consists of 7 members appointed by the
Parliament for a seven-year term, including 3 members belonging
to the executive structure of the National Bank: the governor,
the ﬁrst deputy-governor, a deputy governor and 4 non-salaried
members of the NBM. The Chairman of the Supervisory Board is
the Governor of the National Bank.
In exercising its powers attributed by law, the Supervisory Board
(i) approves the annual report and the annual ﬁnancial statements;
(ii) adopts the internal control system standards, continuously
checks and assesses the operation of the internal control system;
(iii) adopts the rules of professional ethics; (iv) determines the


114
Annual Report (NBM, 2019)
remuneration fund of the National Bank, the level of remuneration
of the members of the Executive Board and the amount of monthly
indemnities of the members of the Supervisory Board who are not
members of the Executive Board; (v) approves the National Bank’s
estimates of expenses and investment allowances and monitors
their execution; (vi) selects, on a tender basis, the external audit
organization; (vii) establishes the method in which the National
Bank Committees are created and operated; (viii) determines the
nominal value, the design of banknotes and metallic coins, the way
of putting into circulation and the conditions for their withdrawal
from circulation.
For the performance of its duties during 2019, 12 meetings of the
Supervisory Board were convened and 49 decisions were approved,
with the following issues:
• Approval of the Report of the Audit Committee of the National
Bank of Moldova on the monitoring activity in 2018.
• Approval of the Internal Audit Report in 2018.
• Approval of the Internal Audit Plan in 2020.
• The
Action
Plan
on
the
implementation
of
the
recommendations
and
proposals
of
the
Internal
Audit
Department and the Audit Committee was noted.
• Approval of the reports on the execution of the statement of
expenditure and investment allowances of the National Bank
of Moldova.
• Approval of the Financial Statements of the National Bank of
Moldova for the year ended on 31 December 2018.
• Approval of the salary fund of the National Bank, including
the members of the Executive Board and the amount of the
monthly allowances of the members of the Supervisory Board
who are not members of the Executive Board.
• Approval of the Expenditure Estimate and Investment
Allowances of the National Bank of Moldova for 2020.
• The half-yearly reports on the level of implementation of the
projects of the National Bank of Moldova in 2019 were noted.
• Approval of the Annual Report of the National Bank of Moldova
for 2018.
• Approval of the Report of the Supervisory Board of the
National Bank of Moldova on the supervision activity carried
out in 2018.
• Approval of the indicative activity program of the Supervisory
Board for 2020.
• Approval of the Regulation on the organization and operation
of the Supervisory Board of the National Bank of Moldova.
• Approval
of
graphic
designs
and
nominal
values
of
commemorative and jubilee coins, 2019 issue.
• Putting into circulation the commemorative metal coin, 2019
issue.
• Putting in circulation as a means of payment and for
numismatic purposes jubilee and commemorative coins.


Chapter 9. Institutional and organizational framework
115
• Approval of the Regulation of activity of the Risk Board of the
National Bank of Moldova.
• Approval of the Regulation on the functioning of the Internal
Audit Department of the National Bank of Moldova.
• Approval of the amendment of the Regulation on the
attributions, competencies and functioning of the Investment
Board of the National Bank of Moldova.
• Approval and amendment of the internal acts of the National
Bank of Moldova.
During 2019, 3 decisions of the Executive Board were issued
for publication in the Ofﬁcial Monitor of the Republic of Moldova
(according to Annex A.18).
In its activity, the Supervisory Board cooperated permanently with
the representatives of the development partners of the Republic
of Moldova and other ﬁnancial-banking institutions on subjects of
common
interest.
The
Supervisory
Board
promoted
the
transparency of the decision making and monitoring NBM
activities with reference to the legal attributions.
Executive Board
The Executive Board is composed of 5 members appointed by the
Parliament: the governor, the ﬁrst vice-governor and three vice-
governors. The Chairman of the Executive Board is the Governor
of the National Bank.
According to the Law on the National Bank of Moldova, the main
tasks of the Executive Board are: (i) to establish the monetary
policy in the state; (ii) to establish the foreign exchange policy in
the state and the exchange rate regime of the national currency;
(iii)
to
decide
on
the
procedure
of
issuing
the
licenses,
authorizations, permits, approvals to be issued by the National
Bank; (iv) to examine the results of the controls performed at the
entities supervised by the National Bank and the adoption of the
related decisions; (v) to decide the issuance of the claims of the
National Bank, the volume and conditions for their issuance; (vi)
to adopt the normative acts of the National Bank; (vii) to ensure
the implementation of the Supervisory Board’s decisions; (viii) to
plan and organize the current activity of the National Bank.
For the execution of its attributions during 2019, 93 meetings of
the Executive Board were convened, at which 356 decisions were
examined and approved.
The most important decisions concerned the following areas:
• Monetary and Foreign Exchange Policy of the National Bank
of Moldova.
• Regulating and banking supervision.
• Supervision, regulation of ﬁnancial market infrastructures,
and supervision, regulation, licensing of the activity of
providing payment services and the activity of issuing
electronic money.


116
Annual Report (NBM, 2019)
• Issuance of licenses, authorizations, permits, approvals that
according to the legal framework are issued by the National
Bank.
• Preventing and combating money laundering and terrorist
ﬁnancing.
• Monetary issue.
• Financial stability.
• Supervision of the liquidation process of banks.
• Management of foreign exchange reserves.
• International accounts.
• Approval and amendment of the internal acts of the National
Bank of Moldova.
During 2019, 50 decisions of the Executive Board (according to
Annex A.18 and A.19) were issued for publication in the Ofﬁcial
Monitor of the Republic of Moldova.
The Executive Board has continuously promoted the application of
monetary policy tools and measures to ensure and maintain price
stability, ensure the economy with the national currency and the
credibility of the national currency; regulation and supervision of
the work of ﬁnancial institutions as well as the efﬁciency, safety,
accessibility and innovative character of payments.
