Chapter 9. Institutional and organizational framework
117
Audit Committee
The audit committee is made up of 3 members of the Supervisory
Board who are not employees of the National Bank. The audit
committee was created by the Decision of the Supervisory Board
no. 9 of 05.10.2016 "On the creation of the Audit Committee of
the
National
Bank
of
Moldova",
in
the
following
nominal
composition: a) Valeriu IASAN, chairman of the Audit Committee;
b) Alexandru PELIN, member of the Audit Committee; c) Vadim
ENICOV, member of the Audit Committee. The members of the
Audit Committee are approved during their activity as a member
of the Supervisory Board of the National Bank of Moldova. Upon
expiration of membership of the Supervisory Board, membership
of the Audit Committee will also expire.
The audit committee has the following legal responsibilities:
a) monitors the ﬁnancial reporting process of the National Bank;
b) monitors the effectiveness of the internal control and risk
management system;
c) monitors and directs the internal audit function;
d) monitors the independence and external audit activity.
The monitoring activity was carried out in full composition
according to the Rules of Procedure of the Audit Committee
approved by the Supervisory Board.
During 2019, the Audit
Committee undertook a series of practical measures and actions,
meeting in fourteen regular meetings, in which it examined 21
issues. The main topics of the Committee meetings were:
• Examination of the activity reports and coordination of the
NBM Internal Audit Department’s Activity Plans;
• Examination and coordination of the draft Regulation on the
Internal Audit Department of the NBM;
• Evaluation of the results of the activity of the Comptroller
General for 2019;
• Examining together with representatives of the external
audit of the Financial Statements and their compliance with
International Financial Reporting Standards;
• Examination of the procedures for monitoring and controlling
exposures and compliance issues that could have a signiﬁcant
impact on the NBM Financial Statements.
During the reporting period, the Audit Committee developed and
presented to the SB of the NBM a series of recommendations for
improving
and
developing
the
business
processes
and
streamlining the activity of the National Bank, including the
ﬁnancial reporting process of the National Bank; internal control
and risk management system; implementation of an integrated
risk management system; independence of the internal audit
function in the NBM; automation of audit processes as well as the
process of planning, managing and reporting the audit activity;


118
Annual Report (NBM, 2019)
implementation of DAI recommendations; streamlining the activity
of the Internal Audit Department; external audit activity.
Other committees of the National Bank of Moldova
To ensure the efﬁciency of the decision-making process, the
Investment Committee and the Risk Committee operate within the
NBM. These standing committees have their own rules of
procedure which detail the speciﬁc purpose, composition, tasks
and responsibilities.
Investment Committee
The Investment Committee is set up to assist the NBM Executive
Board in reviewing investment policies and strategies for managing
foreign currency reserves of the state. The Investment Committee
consists of 10 NBM employees, including 2 vice-governors, one of
whom is the chairman of the committee.
Meetings of the Investment Committee are usually held monthly,
and in the case of major issues, extraordinary meetings are
organized. The Investment Committee met in 2019 in 11 ordinary
meetings.
During each meeting,
the performance of the
investment portfolio for the previous reporting month, evolution
and forecasts related to the domestic foreign exchange market
and international ﬁnancial markets, the evolution of authorized
counterparty ratings and the risks of the investment portfolio were
discussed. At the same time, as appropriate, new counterparties
were examined and included in the list of counterparties
authorized to execute the transactions. Also, periodically, issues
related to the short and medium term investment strategy related
to the strategic asset allocation were discussed.
Risk Committee
In 2019, the Risk Committee was established as part of the
second line of defense, to assist the Executive Board in order to
ensure objective direct supervision over the management of the
operational risk management system in the National Bank. The
Risk Committee consists of 8 voting members, including one
member of the Executive Board – the chairman of the committee.
The Risk Committee shall meet quarterly in ordinary meetings and
may be convened ad hoc if necessary or at the request of the
Governor or any member of the Executive Board.
In
2019,
following
the
initiation
of
the
operational
risk
self-assessment procedure on business processes, a meeting of
the Risk Committee was organized, where the ﬁrst results of the
operational risk self-assessment on NBM business processes were
reported according to the approved plan, proposals for treatment
measures for the strategy for responding to average residual risks,
as well as the risk proﬁle on the evaluated processes. At the same
time, in 2019 the members of the Risk Committee coordinated the
internal acts developed for the implementation of the framework
on operational risk management of the National Bank of Moldova.


Chapter 9. Institutional and organizational framework
119
External and internal control levels
External control
The external control of the National Bank is performed through
the annual external audit of the ﬁnancial statements, accounts
and registers, performed by an audit company with recognized
reputation and experience in the ﬁeld of central bank audit. At
the same time, the external control is performed through the
external public audit regarding the legality and regularity of the
estimates of expenditures and investment allowances, performed
by the Court of Accounts, being limited, according to Law on the
National Bank of Moldova no.548-XIII of 21 July 1995, to examine
the operational efﬁciency of decisions taken by the management of
the National Bank, except for those related to the implementation
of the monetary and foreign exchange policy of the National Bank
and the management of state foreign exchange reserves.
Internal control
The National Bank aligns the governance of the internal control
system
and
operational
risk
management
to
international
standards COSO55, ISO 3100056, to Good Practice in the Field
(including the International Operational Risk Working Group
(IORWG), the National Bank is a member of this group.
With the approval of
the Risk Management Framework in the
National Bank of Moldova, the Supervisory Board established the
appetite/tolerance of risk in the National Bank.
Internal control as well as risk management within the NBM are
established according to the three lines of defence.
The ﬁrst line of defence manages the risks and is represented by
the NBM subdivisions, which are also the "owners" of the risks.
The heads of the subdivisions (line managers) are responsible for
identifying and managing the risks inherent in the business
processes and the IT systems they own, properly establishing the
control activities in the risk management process. For operational
risks, in 2019 a new methodology for their assessment was
applied, according to which each structural subdivision of the
National Bank performs the procedure for self-assessment of
operational risks, with the identiﬁcation and assessment of risks
by business processes, as well as the establishment of control
measures to keep the risks in the tolerance zone, with the
reporting of the results to the operational risk management
function. The subdivisions apply a set of control procedures on a
daily basis to the business processes and systems they manage,
ensuring their effectiveness and efﬁciency.
The second line of defence consists in organizing the process,
ensuring
the
methodological
framework,
coordinating
and
independently
supervising
the
internal
control
and
risk
management process. The functions of the second line of defence
include speciﬁc monitoring mechanisms and processes to exercise
55The Committee of Sponsoring Organizations of the Treadway Commission
56ISO 31000 – Risk Management – Guidelines, International Organization for
Standardization


120
Annual Report (NBM, 2019)
internal control and an adequate ﬁnancial and operational risk
management system. The second line of defence is represented
by structures with functions dedicated to ﬁnancial and operational
risk management, Investment Committee and Risk Committee. At
the level of this line the methodological framework for developing
an efﬁcient risk management process in the NBM is developed:
the internal framework on how to conduct and control foreign
exchange operations, operational risk management by business
processes, ensuring business continuity and information security,
standards, policies and plans for responding to the incidents,
which disrupts or threatens the operational functions of the
National Bank.
At the NBM level,
there is an incidents
management system where incidents are recorded, including the
measures taken and those responsible for doing so.
The operational risk management function ensured, in 2019, the
elaboration
of
the
internal
support
methodology
for
the
implementation of the operational risk management framework,
necessary
for
the
development
of
the
operational
risk
self-assessment and operational risk scanning procedure in the
National Bank of Moldova. At the same time, it conducted internal
trainings,
organized
and
coordinated
the
procedure
for
self-assessment of operational risks on processes.
The third line of defence provides independent and objective
assurance and is represented by internal audit.
Internal audit
provides
management
and
line
managers
with
reasonable
independent assurance on the adequacy and effectiveness of
governance, risk management and internal controls, including
how the ﬁrst and second lines of defence achieve their risk
management objectives.
It should be noted that in 2019 the
internal regulatory framework on the operation of internal audit
was revised, which is approved at the level of the Supervisory
Board, linking it to the mandatory elements of the International
Professional
Practices
Framework
(IPPF),
developed
by
the
Institute of Internal Auditors (IIA).
Internal control within the NBM also functions through a series of
regulations approved by the governing bodies and ensures the
management of resources in an effective way for the fulﬁlment of
the NBM strategic mission and objectives.
Framework of Compliance and Ethics
Code of Conduct for Employees of the National Bank
The NBM Code of Conduct for Employees operates in the National
Bank, which sets requirements for all employees to meet the
highest standards of professional ethics. To that end, the Code
of Conduct is in place to address the personal and professional
ethics of National Bank staff, including conﬂict-of-interest behavior,
conﬁdentiality, security and information management.
Anti-fraud policy
The Anti-Fraud Policy of the National Bank promotes awareness
and fraud risk management.


Chapter 9. Institutional and organizational framework
121
It aims to identify areas of fraud at risk and to implement
appropriate control measures at all levels of the National Bank,
which will help prevent and detect fraud, monitor the risk of fraud
and promote consistent organizational conduct. Anti-fraud policy
also sets out the conditions for conducting investigations into the
prevention and investigation of suspicions of fraud.
Implementing anti-fraud policy, staff training on fraud risk and its
management, reporting suspected frauds - all set and develop anti-
fraud culture that discourages potential authors and reinforces
the commitment of National Bank staff to combat fraud.
The
responsibility of the entire staff of the National Bank makes a major
contribution to creating an enabling environment for increasing
awareness of the risk and the consequences of fraud.
The governing bodies of the National Bank promote a zero
tolerance policy on fraud and corruption.
Measures
to
combat
money
laundering
and
terrorist
ﬁnancing
The National Bank has established internal rules on the prevention
and combating of money laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing in the
operations carried out. The purpose of these rules is not to allow
the involvement of the National Bank in operations or activities
with risk of money laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing, as well as
to establish requirements for the organization, application and
control of compliance by bank subdivisions of measures to prevent
and combat money laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing.
In this context, during 2019, several actions were taken to
strengthen the bank’s compliance with the requirements for
preventing
and
combating
money
laundering
and
terrorist
ﬁnancing, namely, updating internal procedures in the ﬁeld,
training staff involved in enforcing the requirements of knowledge
of customers, cooperation with the responsible subdivisions in
order to determine the risks and suspicions related to transactions
and the relationship with customers, notifying the competent
authorities in order to determine the suspicious nature of
permitting and registration of notiﬁcations related to external
loans, etc. The system of internal cooperation and reporting has
also been strengthened in order to ensure the centralization of the
NBM’s activities to combat money laundering and terrorist
ﬁnancing, including by informing in due time the member of the
Executive Board, who coordinates the activity, about the identiﬁed
risks, as well as the relevant minimization recommendations. For
high risk operations, the report is done to the Executive Board.
Information security policy
The information security policy of the National Bank establishes
the general organizational context to ensure the achievement of
the objectives regarding the provision of information security and
cyber security within the NBM.
The members of the Supervisory Board and the members of the
Executive Board, as well as any employee of the National Bank,
are obliged to ensure the security of the information in their
possession and to which they have access, whatever its form.
At the same time, the responsibilities in the process of ensuring
information security, information management, monitoring the use


122
Annual Report (NBM, 2019)
and distribution of information, the reporting of security incidents,
as well as the way to investigate them are clearly established and
divided.
Public
access
to
normative
acts,
statistics
and
publications of the NBM
The public access to the National Bank’s normative acts, statistics
and publications ensures a high degree of transparency while
respecting the independence and conﬁdentiality of certain aspects
related to the performance of the National Bank’s tasks. Publishing
various data and analyses related to the tasks of the National Bank,
as well as promoting an open relationship with target groups are
issues of major importance in the external communication of the
bank.
The National Bank pays particular attention to the reverse link
in the communication scheme. Thus, through the regular and
frequent transmission of messages, the National Bank provides
rigorous explanations for its activity and the functioning of the
banking sector. The functioning of the interactive database on the
website of the National Bank provides public access to information,
internal data and analysis and aims to provide accurate, current,
clear and transparent information for creating a public opinion on
the decisions of the National Bank, but also for conducting various
studies and research.
9.2
The strategic plan of the National
Bank of Moldova
2019 is the second year of implementation of the Strategic Plan of
the
National
Bank
entered
into
force
in
2018
with
an
implementation period of three years.
The fundamental elements of the NBM Strategic Plan 2018-2020
refer to mission, vision, values and strategic objectives.
Mission of the National Bank of Moldova
The fundamental objective of the National Bank is to ensure and
maintain price stability.
Without prejudice to its fundamental
objective, the National Bank promotes and maintains a ﬁnancial
system based on market principles and supports the economic
development of the country.
Vision of the National Bank of Moldova
The National Bank of Moldova is a proactive, efﬁcient and credible
institution guided by good governance practices.
Values of the National Bank of Moldova
Credibility – policies, reforms, communication and attitudes that
inspire conﬁdence and security for the public and the markets.
Responsability – continued concern about the economic and social
impact of the work carried out and the decisions taken.
Reliability – human resources, internal procedures and information
technologies that maintain the safety and diligence of the
functioning of the institution.
Professionalism
–
competence,
integrity,
diligence,


Chapter 9. Institutional and organizational framework
123
decision-making, continuous improvement.
Cooperation – teamwork to achieve the common goals of the
bank.
The strategic objectives set for the years 2018-2020 derive from
core business areas while the last objective refers to support and
governance:
1. To improve the monetary policy framework and optimize the
implementation of monetary policy;
2. To strengthen the function of ﬁnancial stability and macro-
prudential management;
3. To strengthen the banking sector and implement risk-based
supervision;
4. To enhance the quality of the national currency and the level
of use of cashless payments;
5. To strengthen institutional capacity and efﬁciency.
During 2019, achievements were made in order to implement
reforms in the ﬁve key areas outlined in the Strategic Plan 2018-
2020, namely: monetary policy and ﬁnancial markets, ﬁnancial
stability, bank supervision, payment systems and cash operations,
governance and support. The achievements refer to the strategic
objectives of the Strategic Plan 2018-2020, some of the most
important being included in the following table. Also, in the Annex
A.25 Evolution of major NBM projects during 2019 there are found
the most important achievements related to the NBM projects,
which derive from the strategic objectives.

based supervision 
 Implementation of the Law on the Activity of Banks, which transposes the regulatory 
framework of the European Union aligned with the international regulations Basel III and 
the approval by the Executive Board of the NBM of a series of regulations subordinated to 
the law on capital requirements, large exposures, liquidity, management framework of 
banking activity, risk management, etc. The implementation of the new regulatory 
framework aims to strengthen the domestic banking system and as a result protect the 
interests of depositors. 
 Strengthen the external supervisory framework by approving the Supervisory Review and 
Evaluation Process of the activity of banks (SREP). 
 Strengthen the NBM's internal banking supervision framework by approving the Internal 
Procedure for Banking Supervision, specific procedures for assessing risks during on-site 
inspection and the Methodology for assessing capital adequacy, based on European best 
practices. 
 Initiation of the use of the Information System of the National Bank of Moldova regarding 
licensing, authorization and notification for the transmission by banks of internal 
regulations, as well as their electronic storage; notification to the NBM by banks regarding 
the opening / closing of subsidiaries and secondary offices of banks; presentation of 
approaches / notifications related to payment systems with payment cards, money 
remittance systems or automated remote service systems. 
 Adoption by the MONEYVAL Committee (Council of Europe) of the Report on the 
evaluation of the national system for preventing and combating money laundering and 
terrorist financing of the Republic of Moldova in the 5th evaluation round, following the

Chapter 9. Institutional and organizational framework
125


126
Annual Report (BNM, 2019)
9.3
Management and organization
 
  
  
 
 
The consolidation 
of institutional 
capacity and 
efficiency
The 
implementation of 
a new integrated 
human resource 
management 
system
According to the Strategic Plan of the National Bank of Moldova for
2018-2020, out of the 5 strategic objectives, one is related to the
human resources ﬁeld, namely:
To strengthen institutional capacity and efﬁciency, which implies
the streamlining of NBM’s operational activity through institutional
modernization and strengthening of internal capacities in order to
achieve the core competencies. One of the operational objectives
deriving from the strategic one is the implementation of a new
integrated human resource management system.
Respectively,
the
key
priorities
in
the
management
and
development of human resources in 2019 were focused on these
areas, the undertaken measures being further detailed.
Organizational evolutions
In 2019,
there were several changes in the organizational
structure of the National Bank of Moldova, meant to contribute to
the achievement of the Bank’s strategic objectives and its core
competencies, but also to increase its efﬁciency by reducing
hierarchical levels.
At the same time, these changes aim to
increase the capacity of the NBM to adapt to changes in the
economic environment by redeﬁning responsibilities, restructuring
activities, and strengthening institutional capacity in the long run.
Thus, the internal audit function was optimized, by ﬂattening the
organizational structure,
but also the centralization of the
operational audit function. At the same time, several executive
functions have been adapted to the current needs of the
subdivisions, without impact on the total number of staff units.
As a result, compared to 2018, there is a slight decrease in the
number of managerial positions of the bank by about 1 percent, the
control area being 4.7 executors per manager, slightly increasing
by 0.2 units compared to 2018. It is worth mentioning that the
ratio between the number of units related to the core attributions
of the NBM compared to the number of units related to the support
and governance area in 2019 remained similar to that of 2018,
being 54.0% to 46.0%.
The optimizations made during 2019 had a positive impact on the
efﬁcient capitalization of the bank’s staff resources, emphasizing
the consolidation of central bank responsibilities, extended in
2018, such as regulation, supervision, banking resolution, ﬁnancial
stability, combating money laundering and terrorist ﬁnancing, etc.

The implementation of the new salary system and other measures
related to the implementation of the integrated human resources
management system in the NBM led to the stabilization of human
resources processes in 2019.
Statistical information
In 2019, the voluntary staff turnover was 5.45%, decreasing
compared to 2018 when it recorded a level of 7.48%. The turnover
shows a decreasing trend, largely due to the implementation of the
new salary system, so one year after implementation (July 2019)
the total turnover is decreasing by about 3.0%. Analyzing the
voluntary turnover on hierarchical levels, for managerial positions
in 2019 it was minor, amounting to 0.98%, a signiﬁcant decrease
compared to 2018, when this rate was 4.93%.
Voluntary turnover for executive positions amounted to 6.88% in
2019, down from 2018, when it amounted to 8.3%. We mention
that the voluntary turnover includes only people who resigned
on their own initiative, from the calculation being excluded the
persons employed seasonally and those who left for other reasons
(retirement, termination of the employment contract, reduction
of staff, etc.).
Additionally, for increased relevance, for 2019
and, for comparability, the data for previous years were also
adjusted, the resignations of employees in a certain year, whose
employment contracts were suspended in the previous years, were
considered as resignations of the year in which they suspended
their employment contract.
The average age of the bank’s employees as of 31 December
2019 was 40 years and 6 months. Almost two thirds are women
(61.72%) and more than one third (38.28%) are men. We also
mention the fact that women are represented at a rate of 43.48% in
the composition of management positions, including the Executive
Board.

Performance management
The Performance Management System is an instrument applied by
the National Bank of Moldova since 2016 to increase the efﬁciency
of employees’ work. Thus, following the evaluation of the results
of the employees’ activity for 2019, an increasing evolution was
found compared to 2017 and 2018 of the performance level of the
NBM employees.
Corporate culture
Throughout the year, there were organized events aiming to
strengthen corporate culture and employee retention, such as:
organizing sports activities and encouraging employees to
participate in charity events held during the "Easter Fair",
"Autumn Fair", "Winter Fair", the "Christmas Caravan", which
contributed to the social responsibility of employees and to bring
up the civic spirit. In addition, in the context of the A tree for our
survival campaign, the NBM contributed to greening the city by
planting trees to have a greener city and cleaner air.
Given that the NBM also encourages the personal qualities of
employees, for this purpose a competition was organized to
nominate colleagues, who in the opinion of employees have
special qualities.
At the same time, people who obtained
performances during the year, which are not necessarily related
to the service attributions, such as providing technical assistance
to other central banks, internal knowledge transfer, outstanding
sports performances, etc. were appreciated.

9.5
Internal audit
The overall purpose of DAI’s activity is to provide assurance and
independent and objective advisory services, designed to add
value and contribute to the improvement of the NBM’s activities.
The mission of the internal audit is to enhance and protect the
values of the NBM by providing objective assurance based on
risk, advice and perspective approach. The internal audit assists
the NBM in achieving its objectives through a systematic and
disciplined approach to assessing and improving the effectiveness
of governance, risk management and control processes.
DAI is guided in its work by the mandatory elements of the
International Professional Practices Framework (IPPF) developed
by the Institute of Internal Auditors (IIA) including the Basic
Principles of Professional Practice in Internal Audit, Code of Ethics
of the Institute of Internal Auditors (hereinafter Code of Ethics),
International Standards for Professional Practice in Internal Audit
and Deﬁnition of Internal Audit (hereinafter IIA Standards). DAI
operates based on the normative framework57,
aligned to
Supplemental Guidance "Model Internal Audit Activity Charter"
prepared by the Institute of Internal Auditors (IIA)58. Based on
57Regulation on the functioning of the Internal Audit Department of the National
Bank of Moldova, approved by the DSB of the NBM no. 32 of 12.12.2019.
58Supplemental Guidance "Model Internal Audit Activity Charter" Institute of
Internal Auditors, March 2017.


130
Annual Report (BNM, 2019)
good practices in the ﬁeld, the independence of IA is achieved
through the functional subordination of the Supervisory Board of
the
NBM/Audit
Committee
(AC),
and
the
administrative
subordination to the Governor of the National Bank of Moldova.
In 2019, the audit activity was carried out according to the
approved
audit
plan,
which
was
developed
applying
the
risk-based approach, and the priorities set. DAI in its activity did
not
encounter
any
limitations
in
the
presentation
of
the
independent opinion. The Director of DAI regularly reported the
results of the audit activity to the NBM’s governing bodies: the
Audit Committee and the Supervisory Board.
The annual internal audit plan for 2019 was fulﬁlled to a 100%
accomplishment59.
The Internal Audit Department performed
assurance missions, endorsements of the ﬁnancial statements and
execution of the NBM budget, veriﬁcation and monitoring activities
on high risk areas.
Financial/compliance/operational audits were performed (60.0%),
performance audit (13.0%),
IT audit missions (20.0%) and
integrated audit60 (7.0%). The audit activities covered 75.0% of
the processes assessed by DAI as high residual risk and 16.0%
medium
risk.
In
2019,
DAI
ofﬁcials
participated
as
technical-analytical consultants, monitoring and counseling in 3
major projects of the NBM.
According to the Qualiﬁcation Standards61 the quality assurance
and improvement program has the role of ensuring an evaluation of
the compliance of the internal audit activity with the IIA Standards,
as well as the evaluation of the application of the Code of Ethics.
The external evaluation of the IA function, preceded by the internal
self-assessment, is performed once every 5 years, and took place
in 2015, the next evaluation being planned to be performed in
2020-2021. As a result of the evaluation, the IA function received
the degree "General Compliance", which is the highest degree of
compliance assigned.
During 2019, the process of self-assessment of the internal audit
function took place, this being an element of the Quality Assurance
and Improvement Program. The evaluation period was 2018-2019.
Also, the self-assessment allows the preparation of information
support for the external evaluation of the quality of the internal
audit activity, in accordance with the International Standards
for the Professional Practice of Internal Audit, scheduled to be
carried out in 2020. Based on the self-assessment, the degree of
compliance of Internal Audit with the International Standards for
the Professional Practice of Internal Audit and the Code of Ethics
of the Institute of Internal Auditors is "General Compliance", which
means that the relevant structures, policies and procedures of the
activity, as well as the processes through which they are applied,
meet the requirements of the individual standard or elements of
the Code of Ethics in all signiﬁcant respects.

Chapter 9. Institutional and organizational framework
131
9.6
Information technology
The key activities in the ﬁeld of Information and Communication
Technology (hereinafter ICT) focused on the implementation of
the ICT strategy, which provides for about 21 initiatives, aiming
at 6 strategic objectives for the development of ICT of the NBM.
Following the detailed action plan approved for this purpose, during
2019 about 15 of the 21 strategic initiatives were requested. Next
we aim to describe the most important of the achievements.
In addition to major projects at the level of the NBM, which are
described in a separate chapter, we will note some of the initiatives
of digital transformation of the institution, such as: implementation
of electronic interaction ﬂows with the e-invoice service, extension
of the number and sources of documents that are electronically
archived in the NBM, initiation of the modernization project of the
internal electronic document management system, etc.
At the same time, a number of projects were initiated in order to
strengthen the information security and cyber protection capacity
of the NBM. For example, these projects aim to strengthen
protection measures at the level of the system for international
transfers through the SWIFT network, improve the level of
protection through multifactorial authentication at the level of the
NBM Information System, maximize the security level in the NBM
relations with third parties serving the institution’s IT systems.
Also, in order to meet the development needs of the institution,
several important projects have been carried out to strengthen
the ICT infrastructure of the NBM, which have brought important
improvements in terms of performance and resilience capabilities,
which are imperative to ensure quality, security and the continuity
of the provision of ICT services, critical for the country’s ﬁnancial
sector.


132
Annual Report (BNM, 2019)
Chapter 10
Analysis of the ﬁnancial situation for
2019
10.1
General considerations
In accordance with the Law on the National Bank of Moldova no.
548/1995 (republished in the Ofﬁcial Monitor of the Republic of
Moldova, 2015, no. 297-300, Art. 554), the fundamental objective
of the National Bank of Moldova is to ensure and maintain price
stability.
The basic tasks of the National Bank of Moldova are established in
the Law on the National Bank of Moldova. In accordance with the
legal provisions, the operations of the National Bank of Moldova
are performed in order to promote and maintain the monetary
and foreign exchange policy in the state, to ensure stability of the
banking system, issuance of national currency, management of
currency reserves of the state, provision of payment services and
issuance of electronic money, etc. Respectively, the maximization
of the proﬁt from the activity of the National Bank of Moldova is
not an end in itself and cannot serve as an impediment to the
achievement of the core objective and attributions.
10.2
Available proﬁt for distribution
The ﬁnancial result of the National Bank of Moldova depends
directly on the necessity to perform monetary and foreign
exchange operations in order to achieve the fundamental
objective and fulﬁllment of the Bank’s core tasks. Evolution of
available proﬁt for distribution during the period 2017-2019 is
reﬂected in chart 10.1.
On 31 December, 2019, the Bank recorded a proﬁt available for
Chart 10.1:
Evolution of available proﬁt for total
distribution/losses (MDL million)
-95.31
249.13
436.84
Source: NBM
distribution of MDL 436.84 million (2018: MDL 249.13 million),
which was distributed for the increase of the general reserve in
order to supplement it to the level of 2/3 of the statutory capital.
The main factors that signiﬁcantly inﬂuenced the result of 2019
were:
• The
increase
in
revenues
from
foreign
exchange
reserves management by about MDL 164.61 million,
due to the cumulative effect of increasing the proﬁtability of
average foreign exchange reserves from 1.57% in 2018 to
1.71% in 2019, as well as currency depreciation compared to


Chapter 10. Analysis of the ﬁnancial situation for 2019
133
the reference currencies, especially the US dollar, the
average ofﬁcial exchange rate in 2019 being MDL 17.58 for 1
US dollar, compared to MDL 16.80 for 1 US dollar in 2018. At
the same time, during 2019, it was registered the average
annual volume of international reserves by about USD 3.46
million (from USD 2,927.74 million in 2018 to USD 2,931.20
million in 2019).
• The decrease in sterilization expenditures through
NBC by about MDL 164 million, which is explained by the
decrease of excessive liquidity, implicitly of the average
balance of NBC, due to the increase of the volume of
required reserves as a consequence of the intensiﬁcation of
the crediting process and attracting deposits in the national
currency.
Respectively, the effect of these savings was
partially offset by the increase of the required reserves
remuneration expenses by about MDL 88 million.
Impact on implementation of the monetary policy and of
activities related to relations with the Government of the
Republic of Moldova on the ﬁnancial situation of the
National Bank of Moldova
During 2019, the National Bank of Moldova continued to utilize
ﬁnancial tools to absorb excess liquidity.
Expenditures from
sterilization operations performed through issuance of certiﬁcates
of the National Bank of Moldova have decreased, amounting to
about MDL 380 million in 2019 compared to about MDL 544
million in 2018, as a result of the decrease in the balance of
certiﬁcates issued for liquidity sterilization. Thus, in 2019, the
average daily balance of certiﬁcates issued by the NBM amounted
to MDL 5,500.1 million (2018: MDL 8,402.94 million). The effect of
the decrease in the balance of instruments was partially offset by
the increase in the rates of certiﬁcates from 6.5% in 2018 to
6.88% of the average interest rate in 2019. Thus, the net effect of
these
two
factors
was
the
decrease
in
expenditure
from
sterilization operations by MDL 164 million in 2019.
The effect of these savings was partially offset by the increase
in required reserves remuneration expenses by about MDL 88
million, as a result of the increase of the norm of required reserves
attracted in Moldovan lei in September 2018 to 42.5% (from 40.0%)
and of the successive increases of the remuneration rate of the
required reserves attracted in Moldovan lei in 2019 (from 3.5% to
4.0%, as of 19 June 2019, from 4.0% to 4.5% as of 31 July 2019). At
the same time, starting with 11 December 2019, the remuneration
rate of the required reserves attracted in Moldovan lei decreased
from 4.5% to 2.5%, having the effect of attenuating the growth
rate of these expenses.
Remuneration rates for funds attracted in freely convertible
currencies ranged from 0.36% to 0.01% (2018: variation range
from 0.31% to 0.47%).
The set of monetary policy tools applied by the National Bank of
Moldova during 2019 and the activities related to relations with
the Government of the Republic of Moldova generated a net loss
of MDL (216.39) million, smaller by MDL 85 million compared to
the losses in 2018 (Table A.20).


134
Annual Report (BNM, 2019)
Impact
of
management
of
state
currency
reserves
operations on the ﬁnancial situation of the National Bank
of Moldova
The ofﬁcial reserve assets of the state, held by the NBM, increased
as of 31 December 2019, as compared to 31 December 2018 by
2.15% or by about USD 64.44 million (from USD 2,995.19 million
to USD 3,059.63 million), being invested predominantly in ﬁnancial
instruments denominated in USD, euros and pounds. At the same
time, during 2019 there was an increase in the average annual
volume of international reserves by USD 3.46 million (from USD
2,927.74 million to USD 2,931.20 million).
The net revenues related to the management of foreign exchange
reserves and other foreign exchange operations constituted for
MDL 942.92 million, increasing by about MDL 166.89 million as
compared to the previous year, when revenues amounted to MDL
776.03 million. This increase was largely determined by:
• The record of higher interest income on foreign currency
securities measured at fair value through other elements of
the global result by about MDL 144 million, compared to
2018,
following
the
replenishment
of
the
portfolio
of
securities
in
US
dollars
managed
internally
and
the
investment of related ﬁnancial means of maturing securities
(internally and externally managed portfolio) in higher yield
securities (portfolio rebalancing) in the ﬁrst 7 months of
2019.
Following the decrease in the reference rate, the
decrease in yields on securities issued by the US Treasury
was offset by the increase in the share of spread investments
(supranational and issued by agencies VMVS), which had
higher yields.
• The record of foreign exchange sales revenues higher than in
the comparative period (plus MDL 41 million), taking into
account the sales interventions from 2019 in the amount of
USD 222.24 million, compared to USD 115 million in 2018,
cumulating
and
the
effect
of
the
more
pronounced
depreciation of the national currency against the US dollar in
2019, compared to 2018.
The impact of currency reserves management operations on the
ﬁnancial situation of the National Bank of Moldova is presented in
table A.21.
The average proﬁtability ratio for managing currency reserves
for 2019 increased by 0.14 percentage points to 1.71% (2018:
1.57%), mainly inﬂuenced by the proﬁt from the management of

the securities in foreign currency and the investments higher than
in the previous year (Chart 10.2).
In accordance with international best practices as well as with
the provisions of the Law on the National Bank of Moldova, the
main criteria for the selection of reserve assets are the safety
of core amounts and liquidity. Thus, only after ensuring these
two criteria, the proﬁtability of instruments in which the foreign
exchange reserves of the state are invested is taken into account.


Chapter 10. Analysis of the ﬁnancial situation for 2019
135
The evolution of the ofﬁcial exchange rate of the Moldovan leu
against the currencies in which the foreign exchange reserves
are held had an insigniﬁcant impact on the ﬁnancial situation
of the National Bank of Moldova (namely, in 2019 there was a
depreciation of the USD/MDL exchange rate of 0.39%, compared
to 0.25% in 2018) (Table A.22).
Thus, at the end of 2019, following the depreciation of the
Moldovan leu against some component currencies of foreign
exchange stocks, namely: compared to GBP by 4.3%, there was
recorded unrealized income from exchange rate differences from
the revaluation of foreign exchange stocks in the amount of about
MDL 141.44 million. At the end of 2018, the unrealized losses
from exchange rate differences from the revaluation of foreign
exchange stocks amounted to MDL 247.90 million.
10.3
Capital and reserves
The activities carried out by the National Bank of Moldova during
2019, in order to fulﬁll its core duties, are reﬂected in the structure
of the bank’s balance sheet, as shown in table A.23.
The capital and reserves situation is presented in table A.24.
In accordance with Art. 19 of the Law on National Bank of Moldova
no. 548/1995, the bank’s capital includes statutory capital, reserve
accounts created in accordance with the provisions of Art. 66
and unrealized revenue accounts. The statutory capital, made
up from the authorized capital and the general reserve fund
is dynamic and is formed from the proﬁt of the year available
for distribution, from the revenues obtained under Art. 64 (3)
and/or from the contributions of the Government of the Republic
of Moldova, until its size reaches 10% of the National Bank’s
total monetary liabilities. The general reserve fund shall be used
exclusively to cover losses registered according to the result of
the activities at the end of the ﬁnancial year, if a negative result is
recorded.
Thus, on 31 December 2019, the general reserve fund was
increased by MDL 327,338 thousand, up to MDL 2,024,947
thousand.
Consequently,
the authorised capital was also
increased, amounting to MDL 1,012,473 thousand at the end of
the ﬁnancial year.
By 31 December 2019, the level of statutory capital in total money
liabilities amounts to about 5.79% (by 31 December 2018: 5.21%).
At the same time, on 2 April 2020, the amendments to Art. 19
and Art. 20 of Law no. 548/1995 on National Bank regarding the
allocation of the proﬁt available for distribution, came into force.
Considering that the amendments of 2 April 2020 to Law no.
548/1995 on National Bank also apply to the ﬁnancial result
registered on 31 December 2019, on the same date the level
of capital being higher than 4.0% of the bank’s monetary liabilities,
the bank reallocated, in 2020, 50.0% of the proﬁt available for
distribution in the state budget, registering the ﬁnancial liability
and adjusting the statutory capital so that the authorized capital
is 1/3, and the general reserve fund is 2/3 of the total adjusted
statutory capital.


136
Annual Report (BNM, 2019)
Chapter 11
Research activity
In 2019, the research activity within the National Bank of Moldova
was primarily focused on the theoretical and practical support
of other subdivisions. In addition to the planned activities, the
substantiation of decisions and processes has created positive
premises for increasing the number of requests from subdivisions
on the provision of research, studies and/or analyzes that were
largely unprecedented.
Support for the substantiation of the
decision-making process is the main role of the subdivision of
economic and applied research.
During 2019, the Economic and Applied Research Department
analyzed the effect of liberalization of certain capital foreign
exchange operations on the economic situation of the Republic of
Moldova, in short-term perspective, up to one year, and medium-
term, up to 5 years. At the same time, the study on the impact
of full and/or partial liberalization of foreign exchange capital
operations on the ﬁnancial sector was carried out during the year.
Also, in 2019, a study was conducted on assessing the impact of
exchange rate ﬂuctuations on the export of goods and services.
At the same time, several internal studies were performed on the
norm of required reserves under different aspects. The above-
mentioned studies were performed according to the internal needs
to achieve the basic objectives.
Given recent changes and global trends in the development of
alternative
payment
methods
and
means
based
on
new
technologies, the Economic and Applied Research Department
conducted
several
studies
on
the
risks
of
multi-ﬁnancing
(crowdfunding) and the experience of paying the salary during the
reporting year through so-called cryptocurrencies. The need for
these studies was imposed by the upward trend in the popularity
of alternative means of payment based on new technologies.
The complexity of the basic tasks of the National Bank of Moldova
requires the creation of theoretical and practical foundations of
decisions, in this context the need for analysis and studies is
inevitable to achieve the proposed objectives and goals.


137
Chapter 12
External communication
The
National
Bank
continues
to
carry
out
its
external
communication, in accordance with its Communication Strategy
and its mission to promote and maintain a ﬁnancial system based
on market principles and to support the country’s economic
development. Thus, the central bank is open to the public and
opts for an explicit, optimal and transparent character of its
communication, which it addresses to the various target groups.
The timely information of the citizens is oriented towards
anchoring the inﬂationary expectations in the proximity of the
target pursued by the National Bank and towards increasing the
credibility of the ﬁnancial-banking sector.
For a wider information coverage,
the NBM used several
information channels for the general public, including the ofﬁcial
website of the NBM, the media, social networks, organizing public
events, etc. Thus, during 2019, 212 subscribers were registered
to the newsletter of the NBM website, 2,526 statistical reports
were published, over 340 press releases were sent to journalists,
over 700 responses to their requests were presented. Also, 10 live
broadcasts were organized from various events such as the
presentation
of
the
Inﬂation
Report
(4),
the
international
conference "Development of Financial Instruments in the Republic
of Moldova", etc.
It should be mentioned in this context, the
organization by the National Bank of the event: "Commemorative
and jubilee coin – value and continuity", which went beyond the
ﬁnancial-banking approach and included a message to promote
the national cultural-historical heritage.
Thus, the National Bank expresses its attachment to the values
shared by citizens, publishing information on the activity of the
ﬁnancial-banking sector, but also on the achievement of topics of
general interest.
The ofﬁcial page of the National Bank, which shows the institutional
character and the ﬁnancial-banking subject, mainly for professional
target groups in the ﬁeld, in 2019 presented a wider information
interest for citizens.

The National Bank has further developed communication through
multimedia and social networks (Facebook, Youtube, Twitter,
LinkedIn). To this end, a more accessible style has been practiced
for the rendering of simpliﬁed information and the achievement of
an extensive coverage effect among the uninitiated public in the
ﬁnancial-banking subject. At the same time, these information
channels are important for all target groups, which allows the
central
bank
to
broadcast
messages
to
recipients
using
multimedia and social networks more often than the ofﬁcial NBM
website. Likewise, the redirection from social networks to the NBM
website reﬂects the growing public interest in central bank


138
Annual Report (BNM, 2019)
information. In particular, it covers the topic of European investors
in the ﬁnancial-banking sector in our country (2,167 redirects),
the cumulative interview of the Governor of the National Bank for
representatives of several media institutions (1,370 redirects),
commemorative and jubilee coins (675 redirects) etc.
Thus, the National Bank achieves a ﬁrm external communication,
structured on target groups and based on modern institutional
communication principles. Through its communication, the NBM
continues to be open to the public for its transparent and efﬁcient
information during its central bank activity.


139
Chapter 13
Social responsibility
The National Bank is committed to contribute to the development
of the community through manifestations of social attitude that
inspire conﬁdence and security to the public and markets. At the
same time, in the decision-making process, the NBM considers
the economic and social impact as an important benchmark of
performance.
Human resources management
Employee involvement and motivation play an important role in
the long-term success of the National Bank and in achieving its
objectives.
Achieving a high degree of employee involvement and creating
an appropriate organizational culture are the priority conditions
for performance. Thus, the central bank manages, supports and
rewards employees when necessary, correctly and efﬁciently.
Employment
In 2019, 241 CVs were submitted to the National Bank. As a result,
36 people were employed, 6 of whom had work experience or
studied abroad, this fact also contributing to the reintegration of
the citizens of the Republic of Moldova in the country of origin.
Social Contribution
The National Bank contributes to the development of the
community in general and supports education.
17 students
beneﬁted from internships at the NBM, including 6 people
studying abroad.
Thus,
the central bank supports future
specialists, creating the possibility for them to return home and
get a job in the country.
Actions taken in this regard also include internship and career
fairs. In this regard, training is provided at the level of the central
bank’s requirements and efﬁcient recruitment of potential bank
employees. Through its activities, the National Bank opens the
access of young people to professional training in the ﬁnancial-
banking ﬁeld and, respectively, building a career.


140
Annual Report (BNM, 2019)
University partnerships
The partnership agreement with the State University of Moldova
was signed. Within that framework, it will cooperate in order to
organize studies and exchange of knowledge in order to train and
improve specialists in the banking ﬁeld. Similar agreements were
previously concluded with the Academy of Economic Studies of
Moldova and the Technical University of Moldova.
Charity activities
Three charity fairs were organized to provide ﬁnancial support
to NBM employees and children of employees suffering from
incurable diseases.
At the same time, cultural education and
entertainment events were held, meant to warm the souls of 47
children left without parents and from socially vulnerable families
from Bulboaca village, Anenii Noi district.
Also, the National
Bank was with the students from the Auxiliary School no. 7 and
from the Community Center for Children and Young People with
Disabilities, for which it organized two visits to its exhibition hall
and presentations on the ﬁnancial subject.
A tree for our survival
This was the generic of the greening campaign of the city, in which
the employees of the National Bank participated, along with other
institutions. Thus, planting about 170 trees will bring the people
of Chisinau the joy of having a greener city and a cleaner air.
The little town of integrity
Hundreds of pupils and students from the capital visited at the
end of the year the "little town of integrity", arranged at Palace
of the Republic on the occasion of the national anti-corruption
conference. The National Bank participated in the event with an
exhibition of commemorative and jubilee coins from its collection,
expressing its attachment to the value of integrity as an important
theme for the development of our society.
The National Bank will continue to show social responsibility,
adhering to the activities of supporting the members of the society,
oriented towards openness, efﬁciency and development.


141
Chapter 14
Financial education
Financial education is an area of general interest that is expanding
every year to various levels of society.
The National Bank
continued in 2019 the ﬁnancial education activities intended
especially for pupils and students, thus opting for an optimal
effect of spreading knowledge on the subject.
In this context, the National Bank carried out a series of projects
addressed to the target groups considered according to age
categories.
Thus, between 25-29 March, 2019, the NBM has
traditionally joined the International Week of Financial Education,
coordinated globally by the organization Child and Youth Finance
International. During this period, the NBM visited 2000 students of
all ages, as well as students from Chisinau municipality and the
districts of the Republic of Moldova.
They beneﬁted from
presentations of NBM specialists on the ﬁnancial-banking subject
and saw the NBM exhibition hall.
Also, during this campaign, the NBM experts gave lectures on
various topics in the ﬁnancial-banking ﬁeld at educational
institutions, such as: State University of Moldova, National College
of Trade of AESM, State University of Cahul "B.P. Hasdeu",
Financial Banking College, International Institute of Management
IMI-NOVA,
State
Agrarian
University
of
Moldova,
Technical
University of Moldova and others.
On 29 March 2019, during a conference, the NBM in partnership
with the Independent Analytical Center "Expert-Grup" launched
the ﬁnancial education project "Learn! Give Sense to Money". In
this regard, a partnership agreement was signed. The project
aims to raise the level of knowledge of the population regarding
the management of personal income, in order to "make sense of
money". It is speciﬁcally aimed at young people, but also takes
into account the needs of other sections of the population who
actively interact with the ﬁnancial system.
Following the launch of the project, there were held debates
with the involvement of target groups from Chisinau, Cahul, Balti,
Ungheni, Comrat.
At the information sessions organized, between April and June
2019, within the project "Learn! Give Sense to Money" over 400
students participated. During the meetings, it was tested among
them the level of knowledge of the ﬁnancial-banking ﬁeld. The
sessions also included two contests on this topic, the winners being
awarded with sets of coins.
A project that the National Bank carries out every year on 4 June –
the day of the founding of the NBM – is the Open Day, which aims
to promote the activities of the central bank. Visitors of different


142
Annual Report (BNM, 2019)
ages have the opportunity to see the collection of commemorative
and jubilee coins issued by the National Bank, to get acquainted
with the history of the Moldovan leu, the security signs of the
banknotes, etc.
The National Bank organizes ﬁnancial education lessons both at
its headquarters and in educational institutions. For example, in
September, the NBM received a visit from a group of students
– members of the European Law Students’ Association (ELSA)
and from AESM. The subject of the lesson was "Requirements
for prevention and combating money laundering and terrorist
ﬁnancing in the activity of banks", which was prepared at the
request of students. This order of ideas also includes the training
for interested individuals and reporting entities, within the Center
of Excellence in Economy and Finance, within the Memorandum of
Understanding for training specialists in the ﬁnancial and banking
ﬁeld, concluded in 2017 between the National Commission of
Financial Market, the Ministry of Education, Culture and Research
and the Center of Excellence in Economy and Finance.
Also, on the ﬁrst day of school, the representatives of the NBM and
"Expert-Grup" held a ﬁnancial education lesson within the project
"Learn! Give Sense to Money" in front of the 9th grade students
from the high school of the Academy of Sciences of Moldova.
Also, the civil society shows interest for the ﬁnancial education
activities organized by the National Bank on the occasion of the
International Savings Day. In 2019, the event took place between
November 4-8 under the title "Road of money", jointly with the
Independent Analytical Center "Expert-Group" and licensed banks,
within the project "Learn! Give Sense to Money" mentioned above.
The event was attended by over 600 pupils and students.
In terms of ﬁnancial education, 2019 was also marked by the
signing of a partnership agreement with the State University of
Moldova. The parties will cooperate in order to organize studies
and exchange knowledge in order to train and improve specialists
in the banking ﬁeld.
These and other ﬁnancial education activities initiated and carried
out by the National Bank come to conﬁrm the central bank’s option
to support civil society in raising the level of knowledge on this
subject, with the aim of ﬁnancial inclusion.

Table A.13: Investment limits and constraints applied by the NBM in the investment risk management process
Risks
Mitigating methods
Credit risk
* Investing in safe, high-credit counterparties set by the international rating agencies Standard& Poor’s, Moody and Fitch’s Ratings (it shall be
applied the average rating of the three agencies’ quotations, estimated on the basis of at least two quotations).
* Establish a minimum allowable rating of counterparties authorized to conduct transactions.
* Setting limits according to: type, duration and rating of investment instruments.
* Daily monitoring of compliance with investment limits.
* Quantiﬁcation of lending risk, using the default probabilities estimated by the rating agency Standard& Poor’s, for each type of rating and
depending on the residual maturity of the ﬁnancial instruments.
* Monthly calculation of expected credit losses in accordance with IFRS 9.
Market risk
* The daily monitoring of the market risk is carried out by means of the valuation at the market price of the unfulﬁlled income / losses from the
ﬂuctuation of the exchange rates and from the modiﬁcation of the securities prices.
* The market risk related to the investment portfolio is assessed based on the value at risk (VaR and CVaR) methodology, which estimates the
potential ﬁnancial losses, based on set parameters and assumptions regarding adverse market developments.
* The monitoring of interest rate risk and the determination of the sensitivity of the price of securities in investment portfolios to changes in
interest rates is performed by applying indicator PV01.
* Establishment by the Executive Board of the NBM of the normative foreign exchange composition of the investment portfolio aimed at hedging
foreign exchange risk and optimizing the portfolio through an adequate correlation of the foreign exchange composition of assets and liabilities,
as well as through an acceptable investment horizon to ensure continuity of external obligations and the implementation of the state’s monetary
and foreign exchange policies. The normative foreign exchange structure is determined on the basis of an ample analysis of: the foreign exchange
composition of the trade of goods and services, the external debt, the currency in which the interventions are carried out, etc. The foreign
exchange composition of the reserves may deviate within the limits of +/- 10 percentage points from the normative currency structure.
* Establishment by the NBM Investment Committee of the foreign exchange structure of each sub-portfolio and allowable deviations.
* Compliance with the normative foreign exchange composition and the foreign exchange structure of each sub-portfolio within the allowable
deviation limits.
Liquidity risk
* Ensuring liquidity through the substantial share of demand deposits and investments in securities with high liquidity and low risk.
* In the case of indexed investment sub-portfolios, the daily monitoring of liquidity risk is also performed by applying benchmarks depending on
the type of instrument and currency. Benchmarks are market indices, recognized and used worldwide in the benchmarking of the performance and
risks of investment portfolios.
* The investment policy of the NBM allows the active management of reserves, with limited deviations of instruments and duration from the
benchmark.
Source: NBM

Establishment of the single 
Central Securities Depository in 
Moldova, which will carry out 
the following basic activities 
regarding corporate securities,

July 2016 
The Project took place in two phases, 
namely the second phase of the Project 
took place and was completed in the first 
semester of 2019. With reference to this 
stage, during this period were carried out 
and accepted the stages of Training, 
Testing and Go-Live (conducting 
experimental sessions). 
Respectively, at the end of the first 
semester of 2019, the Single Central 
Securities 
Depository 
initiated 
the 
activity with corporate securities, in this 
sense amendments were made to the 
afferent normative framework. In June 
2019, the general acceptance of the 
information system regarding the Single 
Central Securities Depository was signed, 
the 
Project 
being 
completed. 
Post-
implementation 
maintenance 
and 
support activities were initiated and 
carried out.
